<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebras with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients, finite nuclear dimension is equivalent to Jiang–Su stability. More generally, $`\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A_0\otimes\mathcal Z)\le1`$ for every separable nuclear <i>A</i><sub>0</sub>. This proves Robert and Tikuisis's Conjecture (C1) and the nuclear-dimension equivalence in the nonsimple Toms–Winter regularity question.

- **claim-nonsimple-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-regularity)): Let $A$ be a separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients. The following conditions are equivalent:* 1. *$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)<\infty$;* 2. *$A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$;* 3. *$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)\le1$.
  - Explanation: For separable nuclear algebras with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients, finite nuclear dimension, Jiang–Su absorption and nuclear dimension at most one are equivalent.
- **claim-cuntz-criteria** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-regularity-cu)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a>, let $\iota_A:A\to A\otimes\mathcal Z$ be $\iota_A(a)=a\otimes1_\mathcal Z$. The conditions of that theorem are also equivalent to each of the following:* 1. *the canonical map $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota_A):\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes\mathcal Z)$ is an isomorphism in $\mathbf{Cu}$;* 2. *there is an abstract isomorphism $\mathrm{Cu}(A)\cong\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes\mathcal Z)$ in $\mathbf{Cu}$.* *The abstract isomorphism need not be induced by a $*$-homomorphism.
  - Explanation: Under the hypotheses of the main equivalence, regularity is also equivalent to the canonical Cuntz-semigroup map being an isomorphism, or to the existence of some abstract isomorphism.
- **claim-sharp-bound-after-absorption** (bound, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): For every separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra $A_0$, $$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A_0\otimes\mathcal Z)\le1.$$
  - Explanation: Tensoring any separable nuclear algebra with the Jiang–Su algebra yields nuclear dimension at most one.
- **claim-full-signs-tensor-power** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-tensor-intro)): For every nonzero unital complex $C^*$-algebra $D$ with no characters, there is a finite integer $n\ge1$ such that $D^{\otimes_{\max}n}$ contains two full orthogonal positive elements.
  - Explanation: A nonzero unital characterless algebra has two full orthogonal positive elements in some finite maximal tensor power, with no separability or nuclearity assumed.
- **claim-bcw-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-bcw)): For every separable nuclear $D$ there are finite-dimensional algebras $F_n$ and c.p.c. maps $$D\xrightarrow{\alpha_n}F_n\xrightarrow{\beta_n}D$$ such that $\beta_n\alpha_n\to\mathrm{id}_D$ in point-norm, $\\|\alpha_n(a)\alpha_n(b)\\|\to0$ for orthogonal $a,b\in D_+$, and each $\beta_n$ is a finite convex combination of c.p.c. order-zero maps $F_n\to D$.
  - Explanation: Separable nuclear algebras admit finite-dimensional approximations that asymptotically preserve orthogonality and whose return maps are convex combinations of order-zero maps.
- **lemma-full-compressions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-full-compressions)): If $v\in D_+$ is full and $D$ is unital, then there are $y_1,\ldots,y_N\in D$ such that $$\sum_{l=1}^N y_l^*vy_l=1_D.$$
  - Explanation: A full positive element in a unital algebra has finitely many compressions summing to the unit.
- **claim-roerdam-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-comparison)): If $D\cong D\otimes\mathcal Z$, then $W(D)$ is almost unperforated. More generally, if $W(D)$ is almost unperforated, $u,v\in D_+$, $u\in\operatorname{Ideal}_D(v)$, and $$d([u])<d([v])\quad\text{for every dimension function $d$ with }d([v])=1,$$ then $u\precsim v$.
  - Explanation: Z-stability gives almost unperforation of W(D); almost unperforation plus ideal containment and a strict dimension-function gap gives Cuntz comparison.
- **lemma-uniform-rank-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-half-comparison)): Let $D$ be exact and $\mathcal Z$-stable. If $u,v\in D_+$ satisfy $$d_\tau(u)\le\tfrac12d_\tau(v) \quad\text{for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $D$},$$ then $u\precsim v$.
  - Explanation: In exact Z-stable algebras a uniform half-rank gap over all lower semicontinuous extended traces yields Cuntz comparison.
- **lemma-extended-matrix-traces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-matrix-traces)): Let $A$ be unital, let $k\ge1$, and let $\lambda$ be a lower semicontinuous extended trace on $A\otimes M_k$. Set $\tau(a)=\lambda(a\otimes1_k)$ and $L_k=\mathrm{id}_A\otimes\operatorname{tr}_k$, with normalized matrix trace. Then $$\lambda(z)=\tau(L_k(z))\qquad(z\in(A\otimes M_k)_+).$$ No finiteness assumption on $\lambda$ is needed.
  - Explanation: Extended traces on matrix amplifications factor through the normalized matrix trace, including infinite values.
- **lemma-epsilon-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-epsilon-test)): Let $X_n$ be nonempty sets and let $f_{j,n}:X_n\to[0,\infty)$ be given for $j,n\in\mathbb N$. Suppose that for every $m$ and $\delta>0$ there is $(x_n)\in\prod_nX_n$ with $\lim_{n\to\omega}f_{j,n}(x_n)<\delta$ for $1\le j\le m$. Then there is a single $(x_n)$ for which all these ultralimits are zero.
  - Explanation: Countably many approximately solvable ultralimit conditions can be solved exactly by a single diagonal sequence.
- **lemma-bounded-witness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-bounded-witness)): Let $u,y\in D_+$ and $0\le a<b$. If $u\precsim(y-b)_+$, then for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $r\in D$ such that $$\\|u-r^*(y-a)_+r\\|<\varepsilon, \qquad \\|r\\|^2\le\frac{\\|u\\|+\varepsilon}{b-a}.$$ Here $(y-0)_+=y$. In particular the bound is uniform in a family of contractions $u$ with a common gap $b-a>0$.
  - Explanation: A spare spectral cut gives Cuntz witnesses with explicit norm bound, uniform over contractions with a common gap.
- **claim-gabe-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-domination)): Let $D$ be exact and let $\Phi,\Psi:D\to E$ be nuclear completely positive maps, with $\Psi$ of order zero. Suppose $$\Phi(a)\in\operatorname{Ideal}_E(\Psi(a))\qquad(a\in D_+).$$ For every finite $\mathcal F\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there are $y_1,\ldots,y_N\in E$ such that $$\left\\|\Phi(a)-\sum_{l=1}^Ny_l^*\Psi(a)y_l\right\\|<\varepsilon \qquad(a\in\mathcal F).$$
  - Explanation: Pointwise ideal containment of a nuclear map in an order-zero nuclear map gives approximate domination by finite sums of compressions.
- **lemma-relative-commutant-unitary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prelim-unitary)): Let $D_n$ be separable unital $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras, put $D_\omega=\prod_\omega D_n$, and let $A$ be separable and unital. For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\rho:A\to D_\omega$ set $$C=D_\omega\cap\rho(A)'\cap\{1-\rho(1)\}^{\perp}.$$ Assume that $C$ is full in $D_\omega$ and that every full hereditary subalgebra $E\subset C$ has the following property: whenever $x\in E$ admits totally full $e_l,e_r\in E_+$ with $e_lx=xe_r=0$, there is a full $s\in E$ satisfying $sx=xs=0$. If $h_0,h_1\in C_+$ are totally full contractions and $$f(h_0)\sim f(h_1)\quad\text{in }C \qquad\text{for every }f\in C_0((0,1])_+,$$ then $uh_1u^*=h_0$ for some unitary $u\in\widetilde C$.
  - Explanation: Under a hereditary-subalgebra condition, totally full contractions with Cuntz-equivalent functional calculi are unitarily conjugate in the supported relative commutant.
- **lemma-matrix-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-model-construction)): There are finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of $2$, and completely positive contractive maps $$A\xrightarrow{\alpha_n}F_n \xrightarrow{\beta_n}A\otimes M_{k_n}, \qquad \theta_n=\beta_n\alpha_n,$$ with the following properties.* 1. *Each $\beta_n$ has order zero, and $$\\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\\|\longrightarrow0 \quad(c,d\in A_+,\ cd=0).$$* 2. *For every $a\in A$, $$\\|L_{k_n}(\theta_n(a))-a\\|\longrightarrow0.$$* 3. *For every $n,j\geq1$ there is a positive contraction $w_{n,j}\in A\otimes M_{k_n}$, and there are nonnegative numbers $\eta_{n,j},\delta_{n,j}$ tending to zero as $n\to\infty$ for each fixed $j$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|w_{n,j}-\theta_n(x_j)\\|&\leq\eta_{n,j}, \label{model:retained-norm}\\ \tau(L_{k_n}(w_{n,j})) &\leq \tau(x_j)+\delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b_j) \label{model:retained-trace} \end{aligned}$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $A$ with $d_\tau(b_j)<\infty$.* *The coefficients in <a href="#model:retained-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:retained-trace">[model:retained-trace]</a> are independent of $\tau$. One may take $\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$.
  - Explanation: Matrix models with order-zero return maps preserve the identity in expectation and retain pieces whose traces are controlled relative to support ranks, with coefficients independent of the trace.
- **lemma-corner-trace-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-model-corner-traces)): Let $P,b\in A_+$ be contractions, and suppose that $[P]\leq K[b]$ for a finite positive integer $K$. If $\tau$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace with $d_\tau(b)<\infty$, then its restriction to $D=\operatorname{her}(P)$ extends to a bounded positive linear functional satisfying $$\label{model:corner-bound} \\|\tau\|_D\\|\leq d_\tau(P)\leq Kd_\tau(b).$$ Consequently, for $y,z\in D_+$, $$\label{model:corner-error} \|\tau(y)-\tau(z)\| \leq \\|y-z\\|K d_\tau(b).$$
  - Explanation: An extended trace of finite support rank is bounded on a hereditary corner dominated by a finite multiple of the generator, giving a relative error estimate.
- **lemma-finite-ideal-slicing** (construction, [source](#stmt-model-slicing)): Fix $m,N\geq1$, and let $\mathcal T=\{1,\ldots,N\}^m$. For every $t\in\mathcal T$ and $S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}$, there are positive contractions $(P_{S,t,i})_{i\geq1}$ in $A$ with the following properties.* 1. *For fixed $t,i$, the family $(P_{S,t,i})_S$ is orthogonal; in particular, $\sum_S P_{S,t,i}\leq1$.* 2. *For every $a\in A$, $\\|[P_{S,t,i},a]\\|\to0$. If $1\leq j\leq m$ and $j\notin S$, then $$\\|P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}x_jP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}\\|\longrightarrow0.$$* 3. *If $j\in S$, then $P_{S,t,i}\in I_j$, and there is a finite positive integer $K_{S,t,i,j}$ such that $$\label{model:finite-rank-bound} [P_{S,t,i}]\leq K_{S,t,i,j}[b_j].$$* 4. *For every $a\in A$, $$\label{model:slicing-approximation} \limsup_{i\to\infty} \left\\|\frac1{N^m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\sum_S P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}aP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}-a\right\\| \leq\frac mN\\|a\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Orthogonal families of positive contractions, asymptotically central and subordinate to ideal cuts, average to approximately the identity.
- **lemma-finite-stage-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-model-finite-stage)): Fix $m\geq1$, a finite subset $\mathcal F$ of the unit ball of $A$, a finite family $\mathcal O$ of pairs of orthogonal positive contractions in $A$, and $\varepsilon>0$. There are a finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra $F$, a power $k$ of $2$, completely positive contractive maps $$A\xrightarrow{\alpha}F\xrightarrow{\beta}A\otimes M_k, \qquad\theta=\beta\alpha,$$ and positive contractions $w_1,\ldots,w_m\in A\otimes M_k$ such that $\beta$ has order zero and $$\begin{aligned} \\|L_k(\theta(a))-a\\|&<\varepsilon &&(a\in\mathcal F), \label{model:finite-approx}\\ \\|\alpha(c)\alpha(d)\\|&<\varepsilon &&((c,d)\in\mathcal O), \label{model:finite-orth}\\ \\|w_j-\theta(x_j)\\|&<\varepsilon &&(1\leq j\leq m), \label{model:finite-retained}\\ \tau(L_k(w_j))&\leq\tau(x_j)+\varepsilon d_\tau(b_j) &&(1\leq j\leq m, d_\tau(b_j)<\infty). \label{model:finite-trace} \end{aligned}$$ The last inequality holds for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ satisfying the indicated finite-rank condition.
  - Explanation: A single-stage matrix model approximates a finite set, nearly preserves finitely many orthogonal pairs and retains chosen parts with relative trace control.
- **remark-composite-order-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-model-composite-order-zero)): The composites $\theta_n$ also asymptotically preserve orthogonality. Indeed, the order-zero product estimate gives $$\\|\theta_n(c)\theta_n(d)\\| \leq\\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\\| \qquad(c,d\in A_+).$$ Consequently they induce a c.p.c. order-zero map into $\prod_n(A\otimes M_{k_n})/\bigoplus_n(A\otimes M_{k_n})$, and into the corresponding norm ultraproduct. The same conclusion holds after composing the $\beta_n$ with any c.p.c. order-zero maps into a fixed target algebra: the displayed product estimate remains valid for each composite return map.
  - Explanation: The composites of the model maps also asymptotically preserve orthogonality and induce an order-zero map into the ultraproduct.
- **lemma-order-zero-into-jiang-su** (construction, [source](#stmt-supplies-order-zero)): For every integer $k\geq 1$ and every $\xi>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\iota\colon M_k\to\mathcal Z$ such that $$\tau_{\mathcal Z}(\iota(1))>1-\xi, \qquad \\|\iota(e_{11})\\|=1.$$
  - Explanation: Matrix algebras admit order-zero maps into the Jiang–Su algebra with trace mass close to one and norm-one first matrix unit.
- **lemma-orthogonal-copies-jiang-su** (construction, [source](#stmt-supplies-copies)): For every integer $m\geq2$ there are a positive contraction $D_m\in\mathcal Z$ of norm one, contractions $v_{m,1},\ldots,v_{m,m}\in\mathcal Z$, and a finite positive integer $K_m$ such that $$\label{supplies:copy-relations} v_{m,r}^*v_{m,s}=\delta_{rs}D_m, \qquad 1=\sum_{l=1}^{K_m}y_{m,l}^*(D_m-1/2)_+y_{m,l}$$ for suitable $y_{m,l}\in\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: The Jiang–Su algebra contains arbitrarily many orthogonal copies of a norm-one positive contraction, with a finite compression identity for a cut.
- **lemma-common-part** (construction, [source](#stmt-setup-common)): There is a positive contraction $e\in B_1$ such that, with $q=1-e$, $$\label{setup:common-equations} L(e)=0,\qquad [e,\pi(a)]=[e,\theta(a)]=0,\qquad q\theta(a)=q\pi(a) \quad(a\in A).$$ In particular, if $h=\theta(1)$, then $qh=q$.
  - Explanation: An error contraction in the first subsystem isolates a complementary part on which the model and the original inclusion agree.
- **lemma-localized-rank-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-trace-rank)): For each $j$ there are nonnegative numbers $\varepsilon_{n,j}\to_\omega0$, independent of the trace, with the following properties. Let $\lambda$ be any lower semicontinuous extended trace on $R$, and let $\tau=\lambda\|_A$. If $d_\tau(b)<\infty$, then, for $i=0,1$ and $\eta>0$, $$\label{trace:small-rank} d_\lambda((u_{n,i}-\eta)_+) \leq\eta^{-1}\varepsilon_{n,j}d_\tau(b).$$ If $\ell_n\leq1/4$, then, with no finiteness assumption, $$\label{trace:large-rank} d_\lambda((z_n-\gamma/16)_+) \geq(\gamma/16)d_\tau(b).$$
  - Explanation: The error parts have rank vanishing along the ultrafilter relative to the support rank, while a complementary cut has a definite lower rank, with an infinite-rank case via ideals.
- **prop-localized-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-trace-comparison)): There is a sequence $m(n)\geq2$ with $m(n)\to_\omega\infty$ such that, on putting $$P_n=D_{m(n)}\in\mathcal Z_2,\qquad P=(P_n)_\omega\in B, \qquad \Psi(a)=P(q-1/2)_+\pi(a),$$ the map $\Psi\colon A\to B$ is c.p.c. order zero and, for every $j\geq1$, $$\label{trace:small-part-comparison} e\pi(x_j),\ e\theta(x_j) \precsim (\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+ \quad\text{in }B, \qquad c_j=\gamma_j/64.$$
  - Explanation: With a slowly growing copy index, the error parts are Cuntz below a cut of the weighted order-zero map in the ultrapower, with bounded witnesses.
- **lemma-ultrapower-copies** (construction, [source](#stmt-trace-copies)): For every positive integer $L$ there are contractions $v_1,\ldots,v_L\in B$, represented entirely in the $\mathcal Z_2$ leg, such that $$\label{trace:ultrapower-copies} v_l^*v_{l'}=\delta_{ll'}P\qquad(1\leq l,l'\leq L).$$ In particular these elements commute with $B_1$, and hence with $\pi(A)$ and $q$.
  - Explanation: The second Jiang–Su leg yields any finite number of orthogonal contractions in the ultrapower commuting with the first subsystem.
- **prop-supporting-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-support-map)): There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\widehat\theta\colon A\to B$ whose range commutes with the constant copy of $\mathcal Z_3$ and such that $$\begin{aligned} h\widehat\theta(a)=\widehat\theta(a)h&=\theta(a), \qquad a\in A,\label{support:factorization}\\ [e,\widehat\theta(a)]&=0, \qquad q\widehat\theta(a)=q\pi(a),\qquad a\in A, \label{support:common}\\ e\widehat\theta(a'),\ e\pi(a')&\in\operatorname{Ideal}_B(\Psi(a')), \qquad a'\in A_+. \label{support:ideals} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: An order-zero supporting map factors the model through its support, agrees with the original inclusion on the common part and keeps ideal containments below the weighted map.
- **prop-packing-error** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comm-packing)): For $i\in\{0,1\}$ and every positive contraction $c\in C_i$, $$\begin{aligned} m[(c^{1/2}ec^{1/2})^{(i)}]&\leq[Q_0], &&m\in\mathbb N,\label{comm:small-part}\\ [c^{(i)}]&\leq2[Q_0] &&\text{in }W(C).\label{comm:two-common} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Arbitrarily many copies of the error part pack under the common part in the supported commutant, giving twice the common class as bound.
- **cor-fullness-in-commutant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comm-fullness)): The elements $Q_0,Q_1$ and $H$ are full in $C$, and $C$ is full in $M_2(B)$.
  - Explanation: The common-part elements and the corner unit are full in the supported commutant, which is full in the ambient matrix algebra.
- **prop-commutant-almost-unperforated** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comm-unperforated)): The ordered semigroup $W(C)$ is almost unperforated.
  - Explanation: The Cuntz semigroup of the supported commutant is almost unperforated, via a commuting unital copy of the Jiang–Su algebra.
- **prop-hereditary-condition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conjugacy-hereditary)): Let $D\subset C$ be a full hereditary subalgebra. Suppose that $y\in D$ and that totally full positive elements $e_l,e_r\in D$ satisfy $$e_l y=ye_r=0.$$ Then there is a full element $s\in D$ with $sy=ys=0$.
  - Explanation: In a full hereditary subalgebra, totally full elements annihilating an element on each side yield a full element annihilating it on both sides.
- **prop-functional-calculus-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conjugacy-functions)): The elements $h_0,h_1$ are totally full in $C$. For every $g\in C_0((0,1])_+$, the elements $g(h_0)$ and $g(h_1)$ are Cuntz equivalent in $C$.
  - Explanation: The weight contractions are totally full and all their positive functional calculi are Cuntz equivalent.
- **prop-weighted-unitary-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-conjugacy-weighted)): Put $k^{[0]}=k$ and $k^{[1]}=1-k$, in the constant third leg. There are unitaries $U_0,U_1\in M_2(B)$ such that $$\label{conjugacy:weighted-equation} U_i\bigl(k^{[i]}\theta(a)\bigr)^{(1)}U_i^* =\bigl(k^{[i]}\pi(a)\bigr)^{(0)}, \qquad a\in A,\quad i=0,1.$$ They can be chosen to commute with $\rho(A)$.
  - Explanation: Two unitaries conjugate the weighted model to the weighted corner inclusion for complementary weights, commuting with the order-zero image.
- **prop-two-colour-corner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-corner)): Let $A$ be a nonzero separable unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra and put $R=A\otimes\mathcal Z^{\otimes3}$. For every finite $\mathcal F\subset A$ and $\varepsilon>0$, the map $$\kappa_A:A\longrightarrow M_2(R),\qquad \kappa_A(a)=\begin{pmatrix}a\otimes1&0\\0&0\end{pmatrix}$$ admits a finite-dimensional approximation with two colours: there are a finite-dimensional algebra $F$, a c.p.c. map $\alpha:A\to F$, and c.p.c. order-zero maps $\varphi_0,\varphi_1:F\to M_2(R)$ such that $$\\|\varphi_0\alpha(a)+\varphi_1\alpha(a)-\kappa_A(a)\\| <\varepsilon\qquad(a\in\mathcal F).$$
  - Explanation: The corner inclusion into the two-by-two matrices over the triple Jiang–Su tensor product has finite-dimensional approximations with exactly two colours.
- **remark-two-colours-stabilization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): To pass from the corner inclusion to the algebra itself, we first amplify by finite matrices. Matrix amplification preserves each order-zero return map, and the stabilization accommodates the second diagonal block of the amplified target. The resulting approximations still have two colours.
  - Explanation: Matrix amplification preserves order-zero return maps, so two colours survive when passing from the corner inclusion to the algebra.
- **lemma-stabilization-two-colours** (theorem, [source](#stmt-end-stabilize)): Let $A$ be unital. Suppose that the first-corner inclusion $A\to M_2(A)$ has finite-dimensional approximations with two colours, with a c.p.c. first map and c.p.c. order-zero return maps. Then $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A\otimes\mathcal K)\le1$.
  - Explanation: Two-colour approximations of the first-corner inclusion give nuclear dimension at most one for the stabilization.
- **lemma-gaussian-sign-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian)): Fix $k\geq2$ and $0<r<1$. There are $n\geq1$ and $G\in(\mathbb R^k)^{\otimes n}$ such that, for every list of pairs of unit vectors $(u_j^0,u_j^1)_{j=1}^n$ satisfying $$\|\langle u_j^0,u_j^1\rangle\|\leq r\qquad(1\leq j\leq n),$$ the numbers $$\big\langle G,u_1^{\alpha_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes u_n^{\alpha_n}\big\rangle, \qquad \alpha\in\{0,1\}^n,$$ include a value greater than $1/2$ and a value less than $-1/2$.
  - Explanation: A real tensor takes values above one half and below minus one half on products of sufficiently separated unit-vector pairs, uniformly over configurations.
- **lemma-commuting-factor-products** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-factors)): Let $M_1,\ldots,M_n\subseteq\mathcal B(H)$ be mutually commuting unital von Neumann factors. If $e_j\in M_j$ is positive and $\\|e_j\\|=1$, then $$\left\\|\prod_{j=1}^ne_j\right\\|=1.$$
  - Explanation: Products of norm-one positive elements from mutually commuting factors keep norm one.
- **lemma-pure-state-excision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-excision)): Let $E$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra and $\rho$ a pure state. Given a finite set $\mathcal F\subset E$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a positive contraction $f\in E$ with $\\|f\\|=1$ such that $$\\|fxf-\rho(x)f^2\\|<\varepsilon\qquad(x\in\mathcal F).$$
  - Explanation: A pure state is excised on a finite set by a norm-one positive contraction.
- **claim-imported-central-sequence-results** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-imports)): Let $A\ne0$ be separable with $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}A\leq d<\infty$.* 1. *If no hereditary subalgebra of $A$ has a finite-dimensional irreducible representation, then there are finitely many finite-dimensional algebras $E_j$, completely positive order-zero maps $\varphi_j:E_j\to F(A)$, and elements $c_j\in F(A)$ such that every matrix summand of every $E_j$ has size at least two and $$1_{F(A)}=\sum_jc_j^*\varphi_j(1_{E_j})c_j.$$ This is the matrix-threshold-two consequence of the covering-number bound $\operatorname{cov}(F(A))\le(d+1)^2$ [[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear), Definition 4.1 and Proposition 4.3].* 2. *If $F(A)$ contains two full orthogonal positive elements, then $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$ [[RT](#ref-RT), Theorem 1.2].* *Both assertions apply to nonunital $A$, with the annihilator quotient just defined.
  - Explanation: Imported results: finite nuclear dimension and no hereditary finite-dimensional irreducible representation give a matrix-threshold-two identity in the central sequence algebra, and two full orthogonal positive elements there give absorption.
- **lemma-central-sequence-no-characters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nocharacters)): If $A\ne0$ is separable, nowhere scattered, and has finite nuclear dimension, then $F(A)$ has no characters.
  - Explanation: For separable nowhere scattered algebras of finite nuclear dimension, the central sequence algebra has no characters.
- **lemma-commuting-reindexed-copies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reindex)): Let $A\ne0$ be separable, and let $D\subseteq F(A)$ be a separable unital subalgebra with the same unit. For every finite $n$, there is a unital $*$-homomorphism $$D^{\otimes_{\max}n}\longrightarrow F(A).$$
  - Explanation: A separable unital subalgebra of the central sequence algebra admits a unital homomorphism from each finite maximal tensor power, by reindexing commuting copies.
- **remark-full-orthogonal-in-central-sequence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-2)): The same argument proves that $F(A)$ has two full orthogonal positive elements whenever $A\ne0$ is separable and $F(A)$ has no characters. Finite nuclear dimension enters the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a> only through the two imported results in Theorem <a href="#thm:imports" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:imports">39</a>.
  - Explanation: The argument yields two full orthogonal positive elements in the central sequence algebra whenever it has no characters; finite nuclear dimension enters only through the imported results.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prelim-unitary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_n$ be separable unital $\\mathcal Z$-stable algebras, put $D_\\omega=\\prod_\\omega D_n$, and let $A$ be separable and unital. For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\rho:A\\to D_\\omega$ set $$C=D_\\omega\\cap\\rho(A)'\\cap\\{1-\\rho(1)\\}^{\\perp}.$$ Assume that $C$ is full in $D_\\omega$ and that every full hereditary subalgebra $E\\subset C$ has the following property: whenever $x\\in E$ admits totally full $e_l,e_r\\in E_+$ with $e_lx=xe_r=0$, there is a full $s\\in E$ satisfying $sx=xs=0$. If $h_0,h_1\\in C_+$ are totally full contractions and $$f(h_0)\\sim f(h_1)\\quad\\text{in }C\n \\qquad\\text{for every }f\\in C_0((0,1])_+,$$ then $uh_1u^*=h_0$ for some unitary $u\\in\\widetilde C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prelim-unitary",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D_n$ be separable unital $\\mathcal Z$-stable algebras, put $D_\\omega=\\prod_\\omega D_n$, and let $A$ be separable and unital. If $h_0,h_1\\in C_+$ are totally full contractions and $$f(h_0)\\sim f(h_1)\\quad\\text{in }C\n \\qquad\\text{for every }f\\in C_0((0,1])_+,$$ then $uh_1u^*=h_0$ for some unitary $u\\in\\widetilde C$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.3"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-construction",
        "exact_quote": "There are finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of $2$, and completely positive contractive maps $$A\\xrightarrow{\\alpha_n}F_n\n \\xrightarrow{\\beta_n}A\\otimes M_{k_n},\n \\qquad \\theta_n=\\beta_n\\alpha_n,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\beta_n$ has order zero, and $$\\|\\alpha_n(c)\\alpha_n(d)\\|\\longrightarrow0\n      \\quad(c,d\\in A_+,\\ cd=0).$$*\n\n2.  *For every $a\\in A$, $$\\|L_{k_n}(\\theta_n(a))-a\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every $n,j\\geq1$ there is a positive contraction $w_{n,j}\\in A\\otimes M_{k_n}$, and there are nonnegative numbers $\\eta_{n,j},\\delta_{n,j}$ tending to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ for each fixed $j$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n      \\|w_{n,j}-\\theta_n(x_j)\\|&\\leq\\eta_{n,j},\n           \\label{model:retained-norm}\\\\\n      \\tau(L_{k_n}(w_{n,j}))\n         &\\leq \\tau(x_j)+\\delta_{n,j}d_\\tau(b_j)\n           \\label{model:retained-trace}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\\tau$ on $A$ with $d_\\tau(b_j)<\\infty$.*\n\n*The coefficients in <a href=\"#model:retained-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"model:retained-trace\">[model:retained-trace]</a> are independent of $\\tau$. One may take $\\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-construction",
          "exact_quote": "There are finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of $2$, and completely positive contractive maps $$A\\xrightarrow{\\alpha_n}F_n\n \\xrightarrow{\\beta_n}A\\otimes M_{k_n},\n \\qquad \\theta_n=\\beta_n\\alpha_n,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\beta_n$ has order zero, and $$\\|\\alpha_n(c)\\alpha_n(d)\\|\\longrightarrow0\n      \\quad(c,d\\in A_+,\\ cd=0).$$*\n\n2.  *For every $a\\in A$, $$\\|L_{k_n}(\\theta_n(a))-a\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every $n,j\\geq1$ there is a positive contraction $w_{n,j}\\in A\\otimes M_{k_n}$, and there are nonnegative numbers $\\eta_{n,j},\\delta_{n,j}$ tending to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ for each fixed $j$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n      \\|w_{n,j}-\\theta_n(x_j)\\|&\\leq\\eta_{n,j},\n           \\label{model:retained-norm}\\\\\n      \\tau(L_{k_n}(w_{n,j}))\n         &\\leq \\tau(x_j)+\\delta_{n,j}d_\\tau(b_j)\n           \\label{model:retained-trace}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\\tau$ on $A$ with $d_\\tau(b_j)<\\infty$.*\n\n*The coefficients in <a href=\"#model:retained-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"model:retained-trace\">[model:retained-trace]</a> are independent of $\\tau$. One may take $\\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of $2$, and completely positive contractive maps $$A\\xrightarrow{\\alpha_n}F_n\n \\xrightarrow{\\beta_n}A\\otimes M_{k_n},\n \\qquad \\theta_n=\\beta_n\\alpha_n,$$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\beta_n$ has order zero, and $$\\|\\alpha_n(c)\\alpha_n(d)\\|\\longrightarrow0\n      \\quad(c,d\\in A_+,\\ cd=0).$$*\n\n2.  *For every $a\\in A$, $$\\|L_{k_n}(\\theta_n(a))-a\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n\n\n3.  *For every $n,j\\geq1$ there is a positive contraction $w_{n,j}\\in A\\otimes M_{k_n}$, and there are nonnegative numbers $\\eta_{n,j},\\delta_{n,j}$ tending to zero as $n\\to\\infty$ for each fixed $j$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n      \\|w_{n,j}-\\theta_n(x_j)\\|&\\leq\\eta_{n,j},\n           \\label{model:retained-norm}\\\\\n      \\tau(L_{k_n}(w_{n,j}))\n         &\\leq \\tau(x_j)+\\delta_{n,j}d_\\tau(b_j)\n           \\label{model:retained-trace}\n     \n    \\end{aligned}$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\\tau$ on $A$ with $d_\\tau(b_j)<\\infty$.*\n\n*The coefficients in <a href=\"#model:retained-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"model:retained-trace\">[model:retained-trace]</a> are independent of $\\tau$. One may take $\\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-corner-traces",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P,b\\in A_+$ be contractions, and suppose that $[P]\\leq K[b]$ for a finite positive integer $K$. If $\\tau$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace with $d_\\tau(b)<\\infty$, then its restriction to $D=\\operatorname{her}(P)$ extends to a bounded positive linear functional satisfying $$\\label{model:corner-bound}\n \\|\\tau|_D\\|\\leq d_\\tau(P)\\leq Kd_\\tau(b).$$ Consequently, for $y,z\\in D_+$, $$\\label{model:corner-error}\n |\\tau(y)-\\tau(z)|\n    \\leq \\|y-z\\|K d_\\tau(b).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-corner-traces",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P,b\\in A_+$ be contractions, and suppose that $[P]\\leq K[b]$ for a finite positive integer $K$. If $\\tau$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace with $d_\\tau(b)<\\infty$, then its restriction to $D=\\operatorname{her}(P)$ extends to a bounded positive linear functional satisfying $$\\label{model:corner-bound}\n \\|\\tau|_D\\|\\leq d_\\tau(P)\\leq Kd_\\tau(b).$$ Consequently, for $y,z\\in D_+$, $$\\label{model:corner-error}\n |\\tau(y)-\\tau(z)|\n    \\leq \\|y-z\\|K d_\\tau(b).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $P,b\\in A_+$ be contractions, and suppose that $[P]\\leq K[b]$ for a finite positive integer $K$. If $\\tau$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace with $d_\\tau(b)<\\infty$, then its restriction to $D=\\operatorname{her}(P)$ extends to a bounded positive linear functional satisfying $$\\label{model:corner-bound}\n \\|\\tau|_D\\|\\leq d_\\tau(P)\\leq Kd_\\tau(b).$$ Consequently, for $y,z\\in D_+$, $$\\label{model:corner-error}\n |\\tau(y)-\\tau(z)|\n    \\leq \\|y-z\\|K d_\\tau(b).$$",
      "explanation": "An extended trace of finite support rank is bounded on a hereditary corner dominated by a finite multiple of the generator, giving a relative error estimate.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-slicing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $m,N\\geq1$, and let $\\mathcal T=\\{1,\\ldots,N\\}^m$. For every $t\\in\\mathcal T$ and $S\\subseteq\\{1,\\ldots,m\\}$, there are positive contractions $(P_{S,t,i})_{i\\geq1}$ in $A$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For fixed $t,i$, the family $(P_{S,t,i})_S$ is orthogonal; in particular, $\\sum_S P_{S,t,i}\\leq1$.*\n\n2.  *For every $a\\in A$, $\\|[P_{S,t,i},a]\\|\\to0$. If $1\\leq j\\leq m$ and $j\\notin S$, then $$\\|P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}x_jP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}\\|\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n\n3.  *If $j\\in S$, then $P_{S,t,i}\\in I_j$, and there is a finite positive integer $K_{S,t,i,j}$ such that $$\\label{model:finite-rank-bound}\n      [P_{S,t,i}]\\leq K_{S,t,i,j}[b_j].$$*\n\n\n4.  *For every $a\\in A$, $$\\label{model:slicing-approximation}\n      \\limsup_{i\\to\\infty}\n      \\left\\|\\frac1{N^m}\\sum_{t\\in\\mathcal T}\\sum_S\n         P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}aP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}-a\\right\\|\n      \\leq\\frac mN\\|a\\|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },

        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-finite-stage",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $m\\geq1$, a finite subset $\\mathcal F$ of the unit ball of $A$, a finite family $\\mathcal O$ of pairs of orthogonal positive contractions in $A$, and $\\varepsilon>0$. There are a finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra $F$, a power $k$ of $2$, completely positive contractive maps $$A\\xrightarrow{\\alpha}F\\xrightarrow{\\beta}A\\otimes M_k,\n \\qquad\\theta=\\beta\\alpha,$$ and positive contractions $w_1,\\ldots,w_m\\in A\\otimes M_k$ such that $\\beta$ has order zero and $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|L_k(\\theta(a))-a\\|&<\\varepsilon\n       &&(a\\in\\mathcal F), \\label{model:finite-approx}\\\\\n \\|\\alpha(c)\\alpha(d)\\|&<\\varepsilon\n       &&((c,d)\\in\\mathcal O), \\label{model:finite-orth}\\\\\n \\|w_j-\\theta(x_j)\\|&<\\varepsilon\n       &&(1\\leq j\\leq m), \\label{model:finite-retained}\\\\\n \\tau(L_k(w_j))&\\leq\\tau(x_j)+\\varepsilon d_\\tau(b_j)\n       &&(1\\leq j\\leq m, d_\\tau(b_j)<\\infty).\n       \\label{model:finite-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality holds for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\\tau$ satisfying the indicated finite-rank condition.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-finite-stage",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-model-composite-order-zero",
        "exact_quote": "The composites $\\theta_n$ also asymptotically preserve orthogonality. Indeed, the order-zero product estimate gives $$\\|\\theta_n(c)\\theta_n(d)\\|\n \\leq\\|\\alpha_n(c)\\alpha_n(d)\\|\n \\qquad(c,d\\in A_+).$$ Consequently they induce a c.p.c. order-zero map into $\\prod_n(A\\otimes M_{k_n})/\\bigoplus_n(A\\otimes M_{k_n})$, and into the corresponding norm ultraproduct. The same conclusion holds after composing the $\\beta_n$ with any c.p.c. order-zero maps into a fixed target algebra: the displayed product estimate remains valid for each composite return map.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-model-composite-order-zero",
          "exact_quote": "The composites $\\theta_n$ also asymptotically preserve orthogonality. The same conclusion holds after composing the $\\beta_n$ with any c.p.c. order-zero maps into a fixed target algebra: the displayed product estimate remains valid for each composite return map.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "The composites $\\theta_n$ also asymptotically preserve orthogonality.         "anchor": "stmt-supplies-copies",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $m\\geq2$ there are a positive contraction $D_m\\in\\mathcal Z$ of norm one, contractions $v_{m,1},\\ldots,v_{m,m}\\in\\mathcal Z$, and a finite positive integer $K_m$ such that $$\\label{supplies:copy-relations}\n v_{m,r}^*v_{m,s}=\\delta_{rs}D_m,\n \\qquad\n 1=\\sum_{l=1}^{K_m}y_{m,l}^*(D_m-1/2)_+y_{m,l}$$ for suitable $y_{m,l}\\in\\mathcal Z$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trace-rank",
        "exact_quote": "For each $j$ there are nonnegative numbers $\\varepsilon_{n,j}\\to_\\omega0$, independent of the trace, with the following properties. Let $\\lambda$ be any lower semicontinuous extended trace on $R$, and let $\\tau=\\lambda|_A$. If $d_\\tau(b)<\\infty$, then, for $i=0,1$ and $\\eta>0$, $$\\label{trace:small-rank}\n d_\\lambda((u_{n,i}-\\eta)_+)\n   \\leq\\eta^{-1}\\varepsilon_{n,j}d_\\tau(b).$$ If $\\ell_n\\leq1/4$, then, with no finiteness assumption, $$\\label{trace:large-rank}\n d_\\lambda((z_n-\\gamma/16)_+)\n   \\geq(\\gamma/16)d_\\tau(b).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trace-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a sequence $m(n)\\geq2$ with $m(n)\\to_\\omega\\infty$ such that, on putting $$P_n=D_{m(n)}\\in\\mathcal Z_2,\\qquad P=(P_n)_\\omega\\in B,\n \\qquad\n \\Psi(a)=P(q-1/2)_+\\pi(a),$$ the map $\\Psi\\colon A\\to B$ is c.p.c. order zero and, for every $j\\geq1$, $$\\label{trace:small-part-comparison}\n e\\pi(x_j),\\ e\\theta(x_j)\n \\precsim (\\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+\n \\quad\\text{in }B,\n \\qquad c_j=\\gamma_j/64.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-trace-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "There is a sequence $m(n)\\geq2$ with $m(n)\\to_\\omega\\infty$ such that, on putting $$P_n=D_{m(n)}\\in\\mathcal Z_2,\\qquad P=(P_n)_\\omega\\in B,\n \\qquad\n \\Psi(a)=P(q-1/2)_+\\pi(a),$$ the map $\\Psi\\colon A\\to B$ is c.p.c. order zero and, for every $j\\geq1$, $$\\label{trace:small-part-comparison}\n e\\pi(x_j),\\ e\\theta(x_j)\n \\precsim (\\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+\n \\quad\\text{in }B,\n \\qquad c_j=\\gamma_j/64.$$",

          "source_id": "PM-291.3"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-imports",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\ne0$ be separable with $\\dim_{\\mathrm{nuc}}A\\leq d<\\infty$.*\n\n1.  *If no hereditary subalgebra of $A$ has a finite-dimensional irreducible representation, then there are finitely many finite-dimensional algebras $E_j$, completely positive order-zero maps $\\varphi_j:E_j\\to F(A)$, and elements $c_j\\in F(A)$ such that every matrix summand of every $E_j$ has size at least two and $$1_{F(A)}=\\sum_jc_j^*\\varphi_j(1_{E_j})c_j.$$ This is the matrix-threshold-two consequence of the covering-number bound $\\operatorname{cov}(F(A))\\le(d+1)^2$ [[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear), Definition 4.1 and Proposition 4.3].*\n\n2.  *If $F(A)$ contains two full orthogonal positive elements, then $A\\cong A\\otimes_{\\min}\\mathcal Z$ [[RT](#ref-RT), Theorem 1.2].*\n\n*Both assertions apply to nonunital $A$, with the annihilator quotient just defined.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **nuclear dimension at most d** ([source](#stmt-prelim-nuclear-dimension)): For an integer $d\ge0$, a $C^*$-algebra $D$ has nuclear dimension at most $d$, written $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(D)\le d$, if for every finite $\mathcal F\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there are finite-dimensional algebras $E_0,\ldots,E_d$, a c.p.c.
- **nowhere scattered** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A $C^*$-algebra $A$ is *nowhere scattered* if no quotient $I/J$, for closed two-sided ideals $J\subseteq I\subseteq A$, is a nonzero elementary algebra $\mathcal K(H)$.
- **totally full element** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A nonzero $h\in D_+$ is *totally full* if $f(h)$ is full for every nonzero $f\in C_0((0,\\|h\\|])_+$.
- **spectral cut** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For $\varepsilon>0$, $(h-\varepsilon)_+$ denotes the usual spectral cut.
- **ideal generated by a set** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For any subset $S\subset D$, let $\operatorname{Ideal}_D(S)$ denote the closed two-sided ideal generated by $S$; for $x\in D$ we abbreviate $\operatorname{Ideal}_D(x)=\operatorname{Ideal}_D(\{x\})$.
- **order-zero map** ([source](#prelim:order-zero)): A completely positive map $\sigma:D\to E$ has order zero if $\sigma(a)\sigma(b)=0$ whenever $a,b\in D_+$ and $ab=0$.
- **Cuntz comparison** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For $u,v\in(D\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, write $u\precsim v$ if $s_n^*vs_n\to u$ in norm for some $s_n\in D\otimes\mathcal K$.
- **Cuntz semigroup** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The semigroup $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ consists of the resulting classes, with block-sum addition and the order induced by $\precsim$.
- **W(D)** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Its subsemigroup $W(D)$ consists of classes represented in finite matrices over $D$.
- **almost unperforated** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The semigroup is *almost unperforated* when $(m+1)x\le my$ implies $x\le y$, for $m\in\mathbb N$.
- **lower semicontinuous extended trace** ([source](#prelim:trace-cut)): A lower semicontinuous extended trace on $D$ is an additive homogeneous map $\tau:D_+\to[0,\infty]$ satisfying $\tau(x^*x)=\tau(xx^*)$ and norm lower semicontinuity.
- **rank d_tau** ([source](#prelim:trace-cut)): For a positive contraction $z$, put $$d_\tau(z)=\lim_{l\to\infty}\tau(z^{1/l}).$$
- **c.p.c.** ([source](#prelim:order-zero)): A completely positive contractive map is abbreviated to c.p.c.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-nuclear-dim-regularity | nuclear dimension of A | ≤ | $1$ | Condition (3) of the equivalence |
| q-nuclear-dim-after-z | nuclear dimension of A_0 tensor Z | ≤ | $1$ | All A_0 |
| q-tensor-power-exponent | maximal tensor power exponent n | ≥ | $1$ | Some finite n |
| q-rank-gap-ratio | rank of u relative to rank of v | ≤ | $\tfrac12$ | All lower semicontinuous extended traces |
| q-witness-norm-bound | squared norm of the Cuntz witness r | ≤ | $\frac{\\|u\\|+\varepsilon}{b-a}$ | Cuts with a common gap b minus a |
| q-mean-slice-defect | norm distance of the mean slice from the unit | ≤ | $m/N$ | Integers m and N |
| q-retained-trace-coefficient | retained trace error coefficient | = | $2^{-n}$ | All n and j |
| q-trace-mass-embedding | trace of the image of the unit under the order-zero map into Z | > | $1-\xi$ | All k and xi |
| q-large-rank-lower-bound | rank of the cut of z_n | ≥ | $(\gamma/16)d_\tau(b)$ | Any lower semicontinuous extended trace |
| q-comparison-cut-parameter | spare cut c_j | = | $\gamma_j/64$ | All j |
| q-support-witness-bound | squared norm of the witness r_j | ≤ | $2/c_j$ | Depends on j, not on l |
| q-error-packing-bound | Cuntz class of a positive contraction in a corner | ≤ | $2[Q_0]$ | i in zero or one |
| q-stabilization-dimension | nuclear dimension of A tensor K | ≤ | $1$ | Stabilization |
| q-gaussian-positive-threshold | largest tensor evaluation on a separated configuration | > | $1/2$ | Admissible lists of unit-vector pairs |
| q-covering-number | covering number of the central sequence algebra | ≤ | $(d+1)^2$ | Nuclear dimension at most d |
| q-commutator-defect | distance of the unit from a commutator combination | < | $\tfrac12$ | Finite commutator witness of length m |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-full-compressions** [claim](#stmt-prelim-full-compressions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-full-compressions): Ideal criterion for Cuntz comparison of the unit, approximate compression, then inverse square root of an invertible element. -> finite compressions summing to the unit
- **s-rank-gap** [claim](#stmt-prelim-half-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-half-comparison): Zero-on-ideal trace detects containment; regularize dimension functions to traces by exactness and apply Rørdam's criterion to cuts. -> Cuntz comparison
- **s-rordam** [claim](#stmt-prelim-comparison) / [proof](#stmt-prelim-comparison): Imported comparison criterion for almost unperforated Cuntz semigroups. -> almost unperforation and comparison from dimension-function gap
- **s-matrix-traces** [claim](#stmt-prelim-matrix-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-matrix-traces): Additivity, trace identity and unitary equivalence of diagonal corners. -> factorization through the normalized matrix trace
- **s-epsilon-test** [claim](#stmt-prelim-epsilon-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-epsilon-test): Diagonal choice along decreasing ultrafilter-large sets. -> exact ultralimit solution
- **s-bounded-witness** [claim](#stmt-prelim-bounded-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-bounded-witness): Functional calculus ratio of successive cuts applied to a witness. -> witness with explicit norm bound
- **s-domination** [claim](#stmt-prelim-domination) / [proof](#stmt-prelim-domination): Imported approximate domination theorem. -> approximation by finite compression sums
- **s-unitary-criterion** [claim](#stmt-prelim-unitary) / [proof](#stmt-prelim-unitary): Imported positive-element classification adapted to the relative commutant. -> unitary conjugacy
- **s-corner-traces** [claim](#stmt-model-corner-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-corner-traces): Approximate unit of the corner and lower semicontinuity. -> trace bound by support rank
- **s-slicing** [claim](#stmt-model-slicing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-slicing): Quasicentral approximate units of the ideals, threshold functions, averaging over thresholds, exact lifts by double cuts. -> orthogonal central slices with finite multiple bounds
- **s-finite-stage** [claim](#stmt-model-finite-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-finite-stage): Fix corners, approximate in each corner with convex order-zero returns, take outcomes of a finite probability space, round probabilities to dyadic multiplicities. -> matrix model with relative trace control
- **s-bcw-input** [claim](#stmt-prelim-bcw) / [proof](#stmt-prelim-bcw): Imported convex order-zero approximations. -> asymptotically order-zero finite-dimensional approximations
- **s-matrix-model** [claim](#stmt-model-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-model-finite-stage-2): Diagonalize the finite-stage models along countable dense families of elements, tests and orthogonal pairs. -> sequence of matrix models
- **s-order-zero-jiang-su** [claim](#stmt-supplies-order-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-supplies-order-zero): Path of unitaries into a prime dimension-drop algebra and embedding into the Jiang–Su algebra. -> order-zero map with trace mass close to one
- **s-orthogonal-copies** [claim](#stmt-supplies-copies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-supplies-copies): Order-zero square roots of matrix units and finite compressions of a cut of a full element. -> orthogonal copies with compression identity
- **s-common-part** [claim](#stmt-setup-common) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-common): Multiplicative domain of the expectation, quasicentral approximate unit of the kernel ideal, diagonalization. -> error contraction and complementary common part
- **s-rank-estimates** [claim](#stmt-trace-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-rank): Localized expectation formula, relative trace subtraction, domination on the Jiang–Su leg and ideal arguments for infinite rank. -> relative rank bounds for error and common parts
- **s-localized-comparison** [claim](#stmt-trace-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-comparison): Choose slowly growing copy index, uniform rank gap, rank-gap comparison with bounded witnesses, pass to the ultrapower. -> Cuntz comparison of error parts into a cut of the weighted map
- **s-ultrapower-copies** [claim](#stmt-trace-copies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trace-copies): Coordinatewise copies at large copy index and zero elsewhere. -> arbitrarily many orthogonal contractions in the ultrapower
- **s-support-map** [claim](#stmt-support-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-support-map): Root maps of the model, bounded witnesses independent of the root, diagonalization, extension of ideal containment by approximation. -> supporting order-zero map
- **s-packing** [claim](#stmt-comm-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comm-packing): Approximate domination, packing by second-leg copies, intertwiners in the commutant. -> error part below the common part
- **s-fullness** [claim](#stmt-comm-fullness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comm-fullness): Diagonal domination of positive matrices and corner comparison. -> fullness of Q_0, Q_1, H and of C
- **s-unperforation** [claim](#stmt-comm-unperforated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comm-unperforated): Separable subalgebra, reindexed commuting copy of Z, Z-stability of the tensor product. -> almost unperforated W(C)
- **s-hereditary-condition** [claim](#stmt-conjugacy-hereditary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conjugacy-hereditary): Projection domination, divisibility by copies, almost unperforation, perturbation argument for fullness. -> full element annihilating the given element
- **s-functional-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-conjugacy-functions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conjugacy-functions): Common part equivalence, fullness bound, dimension-function equality, comparison of cuts. -> total fullness and Cuntz equivalence of functional calculi
- **s-weighted-conjugacy** [claim](#stmt-conjugacy-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-conjugacy-weighted): Apply the relative-commutant unitary criterion to the weight and its complement. -> two unitaries conjugating weighted maps
- **s-two-colour-corner** [claim](#stmt-end-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-corner): Unitary representatives and conjugated weighted return maps whose sum approximates the corner inclusion. -> two-colour corner approximation
- **s-stabilization** [claim](#stmt-end-stabilize) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-stabilize): Matrix amplification preserving order zero and compression to finite corners. -> nuclear dimension at most one on the stabilization
- **s-dimension-bound** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-stabilize-2): Strong self-absorption, hereditary permanence and unitization. -> nuclear dimension of A_0 tensor Z at most one
- **s-gaussian-tensor** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian): Hamming packing, Gaussian concentration, union bound over a net. -> tensor with both signs on all admissible configurations
- **s-factor-products** [claim](#stmt-lem-factors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-factors): Induction on nonzero projections and spectral projections. -> norm one of the product
- **s-excision** [claim](#stmt-lem-excision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-excision): Kadison transitivity and approximate identity of a hereditary subalgebra. -> excising positive contraction
- **s-tensor-splitting** [claim](#stmt-thm-tensor-intro) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-excision-2): Commutator witness, pure-state vectors with uniform angle, Gaussian tensor element, excision in commuting factors. -> full orthogonal positive and negative parts
- **s-imported-central** [claim](#stmt-thm-imports) / [proof](#stmt-thm-imports): Imported covering-number consequence and Robert–Tikuisis criterion. -> matrix-threshold identity and absorption criterion
- **s-no-characters** [claim](#stmt-lem-nocharacters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nocharacters): Morita equivalence and order-zero functionals vanishing on matrix summands of size at least two. -> F(A) has no characters
- **s-reindex** [claim](#stmt-lem-reindex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reindex): Bounded central lifts and diagonal reindexing against countable dense families. -> unital homomorphism from the maximal tensor power
- **s-main-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-intro-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reindex-2): Characterless subalgebra, full signs, reindexed homomorphism, imported criterion, plus the dimension bound for the converse. -> equivalence of the three conditions
- **s-cuntz-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-regularity-cu) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-remark-2): Companion criteria for one direction and functoriality with strong self-absorption for the other. -> Cuntz-semigroup equivalences

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-regularity-equivalence**: For a separable nuclear complex C*-algebra with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients, finite nuclear dimension, Jiang-Su absorption and nuclear dimension at most one are equivalent.
- **inv-sharp-bound-after-absorption**: Tensoring any separable nuclear complex C*-algebra with the Jiang-Su algebra gives nuclear dimension at most one.
- **inv-characterless-full-signs**: A nonzero unital complex C*-algebra without characters has a finite maximal tensor power containing two full orthogonal positive elements.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-matrix-algebra-absorption**: Treating finite nuclear dimension of a matrix algebra or other elementary algebra as evidence of Jiang-Su stability. -> The equivalence fails: such algebras have nuclear dimension zero yet do not absorb the Jiang-Su algebra.
- **anti-characters-present**: Applying the full orthogonal signs theorem to an algebra that has a character, such as a commutative algebra of functions. -> The hypothesis of having no characters fails, so no finite tensor power need contain two full orthogonal positive elements.
- **anti-non-nuclear-dimension-bound**: Using the bound of one after Jiang-Su tensoring for a non-nuclear algebra. -> The theorem requires nuclearity and separability; the bound is not asserted otherwise.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence statements about operator algebras: approximations are produced inside norm ultraproducts through diagonalization, lifting and unitary-equivalence arguments, and the algebras are infinite-dimensional and separable. No finite input-output procedure is given, so no runnable reference implementation or finite oracle can decide them.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives existence and bounding theorems for operator algebras. It states no executable procedure that a software library could implement. The proofs rely on ultrapowers and diagonalization, which are not finite computations. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source concerns state evolution, physics or rendering. The objects are abstract C*-algebras with infinite-dimensional structure, so no simulation model follows from them. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool-level procedure is stated. The equivalence theorem is a classification statement about algebras and cannot be run as a checker on a given input, because its hypotheses and conclusions concern infinite objects. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The definition of nuclear dimension quantifies over all finite sets and all tolerances with approximating maps. The constructions use ultrafilters and random rounding of weights. No finite step-by-step procedure with a stated cost is given, so no algorithm can be extracted honestly. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces no representation scheme or container with access operations. Cuntz semigroups and traces are abstract invariants, not storable finite structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No arithmetic, circuit or parallel structure is described. The only matrix constructions are internal to proofs, and the finite-dimensional pieces are not presented as a workload. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no hardness assumption, adversary or protocol. Splitting results for characterless algebras carry no security meaning. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian tensor lemma is an existence argument by probability inside a proof. It concerns sign separation of a real tensor, not learning, generalisation or model training. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimality notion is posed. The bound that nuclear dimension is at most one is a structural upper bound, not an optimisation result. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source has no notion of nodes, messages, failures or consensus. The ultrafilter limits in the central sequence argument are an algebraic device and do not model asynchrony. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only transfer possible is as background for mathematical practice: it limits which regularity claims hold for nuclear algebras. It is blocked outside that setting because a matrix algebra has nuclear dimension zero but does not absorb the Jiang-Su algebra. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-no-elementary-subquotients** (hypothesis): The equivalence of finite nuclear dimension and Jiang–Su absorption needs the absence of nonzero elementary ideal subquotients. A matrix algebra has nuclear dimension zero but does not absorb the Jiang–Su algebra. Blocked use: ['Inferring Jiang–Su stability from finite nuclear dimension for algebras with a nonzero elementary ideal subquotient, such as matrix algebras or compact-operator ideals', 'Using the Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism criteria on such algebras']
- **L-separable-nuclear** (hypothesis): The main equivalence is stated only for separable nuclear complex algebras. Nothing is asserted outside this class. Blocked use: ['Applying the equivalence to non-nuclear algebras', 'Applying the equivalence to non-separable algebras']
- **L-characterless-unital** (hypothesis): The full-signs theorem requires a nonzero unital algebra with no characters. It gives a finite exponent n but no bound on it. Blocked use: ['Applying the splitting to an algebra that has a character, such as a commutative algebra of functions', 'Using the theorem to claim a specific or computable tensor power n']
- **L-bound-needs-z-tensor** (formal_scope): The bound of one concerns the algebra tensored with the Jiang–Su algebra, not the original algebra A_0. Nothing is stated about the nuclear dimension of A_0 itself. Blocked use: ['Reading the bound as a statement about the nuclear dimension of A_0 alone', 'Treating the bound as an upper bound for arbitrary nuclear algebras without tensoring']
- **L-finite-support-rank** (edge_case): The relative error estimates on error parts hold when the support rank of the generating cut is finite. For infinite support rank, a separate ideal-containment argument only forces infinite rank on the comparison target. Blocked use: ['Using the relative rank estimate for traces with infinite support rank', 'Reading the rank-zero case as an absolute additive error bound']
- **L-ultrafilter-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The approximation arguments take place in norm ultrapowers with a free ultrafilter and diagonal choices. They give existence, not an explicit sequence or procedure. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or running time for building the approximations', 'Claiming effective control of the error sequences']
- **L-gaussian-probabilistic** (nonconstructive): The sign-separating tensor is obtained by a probabilistic existence argument over random Gaussian coordinates. No explicit tensor is given. Blocked use: ['Presenting the tensor as an explicit construction', 'Using the lemma to derive a computable exponent or coefficients']
- **L-imported-central-sequence-inputs** (formal_scope): The implication from finite nuclear dimension to absorption rests on two imported results about the central sequence algebra, and finite nuclear dimension enters only through them. Blocked use: ['Treating the absorption direction as self-contained without the imported covering-number and absorption criteria', 'Applying it when the finite-dimension hypothesis of the imported theorem fails']
- **L-abstract-cu-isomorphism** (counterexample_scope): The abstract Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism need not come from a homomorphism. It reaches the canonical map only through absorption. Blocked use: ['Assuming an abstract isomorphism of Cuntz semigroups is induced by the canonical map', 'Identifying a given abstract isomorphism with the canonical map without proving absorption']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebras with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients, finite nuclear dimension is equivalent to Jiang–Su stability. More generally, $`\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A_0\otimes\mathcal Z)\le1`$ for every separable nuclear <i>A</i><sub>0</sub>. This proves Robert and Tikuisis's Conjecture (C1) and the nuclear-dimension equivalence in the nonsimple Toms–Winter regularity question.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Nuclear dimension and Jiang–Su absorption describe two forms of regularity in a nuclear $C^*$-algebra. Nuclear dimension counts the order-zero pieces needed in finite-dimensional completely positive approximations; an order-zero map preserves orthogonality of positive elements. Jiang–Su stability means an isomorphism $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$, where $\mathcal Z$ is the algebra introduced by Jiang and Su <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[JS](#ref-JS)]. The first condition gives a bound on approximation complexity, while the second expresses invariance under tensoring with a fixed infinite-dimensional algebra. Their relationship connects noncommutative dimension with the structure and classification of nuclear $C^*$-algebras.

The obstruction to passing from finite dimension to absorption is already visible in a matrix algebra: it has nuclear dimension zero but does not absorb $\mathcal Z$. The relevant exclusion must apply throughout the ideal structure. A $C^*$-algebra $A$ is *nowhere scattered* if no quotient $I/J$, for closed two-sided ideals $J\subseteq I\subseteq A$, is a nonzero elementary algebra $\mathcal K(H)$. This is equivalent to the absence of finite-dimensional irreducible representations in every hereditary subalgebra <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[TV](#ref-TV), Theorem 3.1].

<a id="stmt-intro-regularity"></a>
<div id="intro:regularity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Nonsimple nuclear regularity). *<span id="thm:main" label="thm:main"></span> Let $A$ be a separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra with no nonzero elementary ideal subquotients. The following conditions are equivalent:*

1.  *$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)<\infty$;*

2.  *$A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$;*

3.  *$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A)\le1$.*

</div>

The implication from finite nuclear dimension to absorption proves Conjecture (C1) of Robert and Tikuisis <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[RT](#ref-RT)]. Together the two implications establish the nuclear-dimension equivalence in the nonsimple Toms–Winter regularity question of Schafhauser–Tikuisis–White <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[STW99](#ref-STW99), Section 22, Problem LXXV]. Here and throughout, unadorned tensor products are spatial. The maximal tensor products used in the independent splitting theorem below carry an explicit subscript.

The companion <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CuntzCompanion](#ref-CuntzCompanion), Corollaries 1.3 and 1.4] adds Cuntz-semigroup criteria to the main equivalence.

<a id="stmt-cor-regularity-cu"></a>
<div id="cor:regularity-cu" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Regularity and Cuntz-semigroup criteria). *Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a>, let $\iota_A:A\to A\otimes\mathcal Z$ be $\iota_A(a)=a\otimes1_\mathcal Z$. The conditions of that theorem are also equivalent to each of the following:*

1.  *the canonical map $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota_A):\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes\mathcal Z)$ is an isomorphism in $\mathbf{Cu}$;*

2.  *there is an abstract isomorphism $\mathrm{Cu}(A)\cong\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes\mathcal Z)$ in $\mathbf{Cu}$.*

*The abstract isomorphism need not be induced by a $*$-homomorphism.*

</div>

The proof, including the nonunital naturality argument for the canonical map, follows the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a> in Section <a href="#subsec:cu-consequence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:cu-consequence">9.1</a>.

The dimension construction has a broader formulation, without a separate assumption on elementary subquotients.

<a id="stmt-intro-main"></a>
<div id="intro:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Sharp bound after Jiang–Su absorption). *For every separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra $A_0$, $$\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A_0\otimes\mathcal Z)\le1.$$*

</div>

The bound is optimal uniformly over this class. Nuclear dimension zero for a separable algebra implies the AF property <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear), Remarks 2.2(iii)], whereas $\mathcal Z$ is unital and infinite-dimensional with no projections other than $0$ and $1$. Thus $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(\mathcal Z)=1$.

The absorption implication uses a separate theorem. A positive element is *full* if it generates the whole ambient algebra as a closed two-sided ideal, and a *character* is a nonzero $*$-homomorphism to $\mathbb C$.

<a id="stmt-thm-tensor-intro"></a>
<div id="thm:tensor-intro" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Full signs in a finite maximal tensor power). *For every nonzero unital complex $C^*$-algebra $D$ with no characters, there is a finite integer $n\ge1$ such that $D^{\otimes_{\max}n}$ contains two full orthogonal positive elements.*

</div>

No separability or nuclearity is assumed in this theorem. Its finite conclusion answers affirmatively Kirchberg and Rørdam’s Question 6.2 , which asks for this splitting property in the infinite maximal tensor power. The passage to the infinite power is by its canonical unital inclusion, which preserves fullness and orthogonality.

The companion <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Fixed324](#ref-Fixed324), Theorems 1.1 and 1.2] obtains another finite endpoint under the same hypotheses on $D$: for some $m\ge1$, there is a unital $*$-homomorphism from the prime dimension-drop algebra $I(2,3)$ to $D^{\otimes_{\max}m}$. For every free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, it also proves that a nonzero unital separable complex $C^*$-algebra $A$ is $\mathcal Z$-stable exactly when its norm central-sequence algebra $A_\omega\cap A'$ has no characters. The proof below uses only the full-sign endpoint of Theorem <a href="#thm:tensor-intro" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tensor-intro">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Background and the two obstacles

Winter and Zacharias introduced nuclear dimension as a noncommutative covering dimension and established its basic permanence properties <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear)]. Their order-zero structure theorem <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[WZOrderZero](#ref-WZOrderZero)] makes a colour into a homomorphism multiplied by a commuting positive weight. Toms and Winter’s theory of strongly self-absorbing algebras <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[TW](#ref-TW)] explains why absorption can supply commuting tensor factors without changing the isomorphism class. In the simple setting, Matui–Sato proved that a unital separable simple nuclear quasidiagonal algebra with a unique trace has decomposition rank at most one after tensoring with the universal UHF algebra <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[MS](#ref-MS), Theorem 4.2]. Decomposition rank requires the same order-zero decomposition as nuclear dimension, together with contractivity of the entire return map; its bounds therefore also bound nuclear dimension. Sato–White–Winter established the bound three for separable unital simple nuclear $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras with a unique trace <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[SWW](#ref-SWW), Theorem B]. The two-coloured classification work of Bosa–Brown–Sato–Tikuisis–White–Winter obtained nuclear dimension one for non-AF separable unital simple nuclear $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras whose trace simplex is nonempty and Bauer <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BBSTWW](#ref-BBSTWW), Theorem F]. Castillejos–Evington–Tikuisis–White–Winter removed the trace-boundary restriction in the unital case <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CETWW](#ref-CETWW), Theorem B], and Castillejos–Evington treated the nonunital case <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CE](#ref-CE), Theorem A].

Nonsimple antecedents reduce dimension in specific structural classes. Tikuisis–Winter proved decomposition rank at most two after $\mathcal Z$-stabilization for algebras locally approximable by hereditary subalgebras of $C(X,\mathcal K)$, with $X$ compact Hausdorff <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[TiW](#ref-TiW), Theorem 4.1]. Elliott–Niu–Santiago–Tikuisis extended the bound two to locally subhomogeneous $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ENST](#ref-ENST), Theorem A]. In the purely infinite direction, Szabó proved $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(A\otimes\mathcal O_\infty)\le3$ for every separable nuclear $A$ <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[SzaboOInfinity](#ref-SzaboOInfinity), Theorem 2.3]. Bosa–Gabe–Sims–White then established nuclear dimension at most one for separable nuclear $\mathcal O_\infty$-stable algebras <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BGSW](#ref-BGSW), Theorem A]. Their homomorphism theorem gives the same bound for a nuclear $\mathcal O_\infty$-stable homomorphism from a separable exact domain to an arbitrary target <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BGSW](#ref-BGSW), Theorem C].

The nonsimple dimension problem has an additional difficulty: a trace may be bounded on one ideal and infinite outside it, and stably finite and purely infinite ideal quotients may coexist. The appropriate trace cone therefore consists of all lower semicontinuous extended traces, as developed by Elliott–Robert–Santiago <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS)]. Our estimates are relative to the support rank of a spectral cut generating the ideal being tested. This rank measures the trace of the support, rather than the trace value of the cut itself. When it is finite, a relative estimate controls the error; rank zero forces the error rank to vanish. When it is infinite, a separate ideal-containment argument forces the comparison target to have infinite rank. The construction begins with the convex order-zero approximations of Hirshberg–Kirchberg–White <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[HKW](#ref-HKW)], in the refinement of Brown–Carrión–White with asymptotically order-zero incoming maps <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BCW](#ref-BCW)]. It localizes them inside hereditary corners, and rounds their convex weights to obtain one matrix-valued order-zero return map.

The comparison stage uses two distinct forms of regularity. At each coordinate, exactness and $\mathcal Z$-stability turn a uniform extended-rank gap into Cuntz comparison, using Rørdam’s almost-unperforation theorem <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Rordam](#ref-Rordam)] and Elliott–Robert–Santiago’s comparison criterion <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Proposition 6.2]. In the supported relative commutant of the ultrapower, we instead use abstract dimension functions. Gabe’s order-zero formulation <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe)] of the Kirchberg–Rørdam cone criterion <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[KR05](#ref-KR05)] supplies finite compression sums from pointwise ideal containment. Packing these sums yields comparisons inside that commutant. Fullness, divisibility in a full corner, and almost unperforation verify a further hereditary-subalgebra condition. The unitary-equivalence criterion of Bosa–Brown–Sato–Tikuisis–White–Winter <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BBSTWW](#ref-BBSTWW)], which adapts Robert–Santiago’s positive-element classification method <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[RS](#ref-RS)], turns the comparisons into two weighted conjugacies.

For the reverse implication, Winter proved that a separable simple unital nonelementary algebra of finite nuclear dimension absorbs $\mathcal Z$ <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[WinterAbsorption](#ref-WinterAbsorption), Corollary 6.3]. Tikuisis extended this to separable algebras of finite nuclear dimension with finitely many ideals and no elementary quotient of an ideal <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[TikuisisFiniteIdeals](#ref-TikuisisFiniteIdeals), Corollary 8.7]. Robert–Tikuisis obtained further nonsimple cases under restrictions on the primitive ideal space and subquotients <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[RT](#ref-RT), Corollaries 7.11 and 7.16], and reduced absorption for separable algebras of finite nuclear dimension to the existence of two full orthogonal positive elements in the central sequence algebra <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[RT](#ref-RT), Theorem 1.2]. Winter–Zacharias’s finite covering-number bound <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear), Proposition 4.3] rules out characters in that algebra under the nowhere-scattered hypothesis. The remaining step is precisely a full splitting construction. Kirchberg and Rørdam isolated the character and tensor-splitting questions in <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[KR](#ref-KR)]. Our finite tensor construction supplies the needed splitting by combining a uniform Gaussian sign estimate with pure-state excision. The latter is the method of Akemann–Anderson–Pedersen <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[AAP](#ref-AAP)]; we include its short proof using Kadison transitivity <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Kadison](#ref-Kadison)].

Recent Cuntz-semigroup approaches provide a complementary perspective. Antoine–Perera–Thiel–Vilalta relate finite nuclear dimension and the Global Glimm Property to comparison and divisibility <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[APTV](#ref-APTV), Theorem 6.5]; Perera–Thiel–Vilalta characterize absorption through the corresponding regularity of central sequence algebras <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[PTV](#ref-PTV), Theorem 3.6]. Our reverse implication instead uses the finite covering bound and the full-sign theorem to meet the Robert–Tikuisis criterion directly.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof overview

The two implications are independent until the final assembly. Figure <a href="#fig:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof">1</a> records their interfaces.

For the dimension bound, we first work with a unital separable nuclear $A$, without absorption. In Section <a href="#sec:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:model">3</a>, quasicentral ideal cuts give finitely many families of hereditary pieces. The pieces are orthogonal within each fixed threshold family, and averaging the family compressions retains the prescribed tests. We fix the pieces and their comparison bounds by finite sums of ideal generators before choosing inner approximation errors. This ordering turns norm errors into bounds relative to the support ranks of those generators, uniformly over all traces for which the ranks are finite.

Section <a href="#sec:traces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:traces">4</a> places the matrix model in $A\otimes\mathcal Z_1\otimes\mathcal Z_2\otimes\mathcal Z_3$. The first $\mathcal Z$ factor carries the model. In the norm ultrapower, a positive contraction $q$ identifies a part on which the model agrees with the original inclusion. The second factor supplies arbitrarily many orthogonal copies of a norm-one positive contraction. Relative trace estimates compare the error part with this common part, and a spare spectral cut bounds the comparison witnesses before they pass to the ultrapower.

Section <a href="#sec:commutant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:commutant">5</a> constructs a supporting map and uses approximate domination to pack the error into the common part. This controls dimension functions in a supported relative commutant without assuming that commutant exact. A full corner, its divisibility, and almost unperforation give the hereditary-subalgebra condition needed for unitary equivalence. The constant third factor supplies a full-spectrum positive contraction $k$. Functional-calculus comparison gives unitary conjugacies for the two weights $k$ and $1-k$. Their sum gives exactly two colours for a corner inclusion. In Section <a href="#sec:endgame" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endgame">6</a>, stabilization and hereditary permanence preserve those two colours, and unitization handles the nonunital algebra. This proves Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">3</a>.

For absorption, Section <a href="#sec:gaussian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:gaussian">7</a> constructs a real tensor that takes both signs on every product of sufficiently separated pairs of unit vectors. A Hamming packing provides exponentially many weakly correlated Gaussian evaluations. Their failure probability decays fast enough to control a net of all admissible configurations. In Section <a href="#sec:tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tensor">8</a>, a finite commutator witness converts the absence of characters into uniform separation of pure-state expectation vectors. Excision transfers the tensor evaluations to every irreducible representation of the maximal tensor product. The positive and negative parts of the resulting selfadjoint element are therefore both full.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:central" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:central">9</a> uses the norm central sequence algebra $F(A)$, with its annihilator quotient in the nonunital case. Finite covering excludes characters; a separable characterless subalgebra containing $1_{F(A)}$ has a finite tensor splitting by Theorem <a href="#thm:tensor-intro" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tensor-intro">4</a>. Commuting reindexed copies give a unital homomorphism from that tensor power into $F(A)$. Fullness survives this map, so Robert–Tikuisis’s criterion gives absorption and completes Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a>.

<tr class="even">
<td style="text-align: left;">$dim_{nuc}(A_{0}⊗𝒵) ≤ 1$</td>
<td style="text-align: left;">Robert–Tikuisis criterion: $A ≅ A ⊗ 𝒵$</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption>The two proof branches. The left branch uses spatial tensor products, first constructs the unital model, and then passes to $A_{0} ⊗ 𝒵$ by hereditary permanence. The splitting theorem in the right column requires neither separability nor nuclearity of $D$; its application to $F(A)$ uses the displayed unital map. The branches meet only in the main equivalence.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-proof"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:proof`)

Left column:

  (later $A = \widetilde{A_0 \otimes \mathcal{Z}}$)

Right column:

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Preliminaries

We collect the approximation, comparison and diagonalization tools for the dimension bound, including comparison from a uniform rank gap for all lower semicontinuous extended traces. The central-sequence inputs for the independent absorption implication are stated in Section <a href="#sec:central" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:central">9</a>.

All $C^*$-algebras are complex. An unadorned tensor product is spatial; maximal tensor products are explicitly marked $\otimes_{\max}$. Representations are nondegenerate.
