We write $D_+$ for the positive cone of a $C^*$-algebra $D$, $\widetilde D$ for its unitization, and $$\operatorname{her}_D(h)=\overline{hDh}\quad(h\in D_+).$$ For any subset $S\subset D$, let $\operatorname{Ideal}_D(S)$ denote the closed two-sided ideal generated by $S$; for $x\in D$ we abbreviate $\operatorname{Ideal}_D(x)=\operatorname{Ideal}_D(\{x\})$. The ambient algebra is omitted when it is clear. For $\varepsilon>0$, $(h-\varepsilon)_+$ denotes the usual spectral cut. A full element is one generating the whole ambient algebra as a closed ideal. A nonzero $h\in D_+$ is *totally full* if $f(h)$ is full for every nonzero $f\in C_0((0,\|h\|])_+$. For a subset $S$ of an ambient algebra, $S^\perp$ consists of the elements annihilating $S$ on both sides.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Order zero and finite-dimensional approximation

<a id="prelim:order-zero"></a>
A completely positive contractive map is abbreviated to c.p.c. A completely positive map $\sigma:D\to E$ has order zero if $\sigma(a)\sigma(b)=0$ whenever $a,b\in D_+$ and $ab=0$. For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\sigma$, the structure theorem of Winter–Zacharias <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[WZOrderZero](#ref-WZOrderZero), Theorem 3.3 and Corollaries 4.1–4.2] gives a $*$-homomorphism $\sigma_{\mathrm{hom}}$ and a commuting positive contraction $h$ in the multiplier algebra of $C^*(\sigma(D))$ such that $$\sigma(a)=h\sigma_{\mathrm{hom}}(a).$$ For $f\in C_0((0,1])_+$, the formula $f(\sigma)(a)=f(h)\sigma_{\mathrm{hom}}(a)$ defines an order-zero map into $E$, contractive when $\|f\|\le1$. We write $\sigma^{1/l}$ for $f(t)=t^{1/l}$. In particular, $$\label{prelim:order-zero}
 \|\sigma(a)\sigma(b)\|\le\|ab\|\quad(a,b\in D),
 \qquad
 \sigma(a)\sigma(b)=\sigma(1)\sigma(ab)
 \quad\text{if $D$ is unital}.$$ The first inequality follows by writing the product as $h^2\sigma_{\mathrm{hom}}(ab)$. Multiplication of a c.p.c. order-zero map by a positive contraction commuting with its entire range again gives a c.p.c. order-zero map.

<a id="stmt-prelim-nuclear-dimension"></a>
<div id="prelim:nuclear-dimension" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Winter–Zacharias <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[WZNuclear](#ref-WZNuclear), Definition 2.1]). For an integer $d\ge0$, a $C^*$-algebra $D$ has nuclear dimension at most $d$, written $\dim_{\mathrm{nuc}}(D)\le d$, if for every finite $\mathcal F\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there are finite-dimensional algebras $E_0,\ldots,E_d$, a c.p.c. map $\psi:D\to\bigoplus_{j=0}^dE_j$, and c.p.c. order-zero maps $\varphi_j:E_j\to D$ such that $$\left\|\sum_{j=0}^d\varphi_j\psi_j(a)-a\right\|<\varepsilon
 \qquad(a\in\mathcal F).$$ The $d+1$ summands are called colours. The sum of the return maps need not be contractive; each coloured return map must be contractive.

</div>

We use the following approximation theorem in hereditary subalgebras as well as in unital algebras.

<a id="stmt-prelim-bcw"></a>
<div id="prelim:bcw" class="theorem">

**Theorem 6** (Brown–Carrión–White). *For every separable nuclear $D$ there are finite-dimensional algebras $F_n$ and c.p.c. maps $$D\xrightarrow{\alpha_n}F_n\xrightarrow{\beta_n}D$$ such that $\beta_n\alpha_n\to\mathrm{id}_D$ in point-norm, $\|\alpha_n(a)\alpha_n(b)\|\to0$ for orthogonal $a,b\in D_+$, and each $\beta_n$ is a finite convex combination of c.p.c. order-zero maps $F_n\to D$.*

</div>

<a id="prelim:asymptotic-product"></a>
The convex order-zero decomposition is due to Hirshberg–Kirchberg–White <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[HKW](#ref-HKW), Theorem 1.4]; the asymptotic orthogonality refinement used here is <a id="pm-cite-43"></a>[[BCW](#ref-BCW), Theorem 3.1]. In particular, the map induced by the $\alpha_n$ in $\prod_nF_n/\bigoplus_nF_n$ is order zero, so $$\label{prelim:asymptotic-product}
 \limsup_n\|\alpha_n(a)\alpha_n(b)\|\le\|ab\|
 \qquad(a,b\in D).$$ Nuclearity passes to hereditary subalgebras and to unitizations. A c.p.c. map from a separable nuclear algebra to a quotient has a c.p.c. lift by the Choi–Effros lifting theorem <a id="pm-cite-44"></a>[[ChoiEffros](#ref-ChoiEffros), Theorem 3.10]. Every completely positive map from a nuclear algebra is nuclear, by composition with finite-dimensional approximations of its domain.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Comparison and extended traces

Write $\mathcal K$ for the compact operators on a separable infinite-dimensional Hilbert space. For $u,v\in(D\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, write $u\precsim v$ if $s_n^*vs_n\to u$ in norm for some $s_n\in D\otimes\mathcal K$. Mutual comparison is Cuntz equivalence. The semigroup $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ consists of the resulting classes, with block-sum addition and the order induced by $\precsim$. Its subsemigroup $W(D)$ consists of classes represented in finite matrices over $D$. We use the following standard properties: $$[u+v]\le[u]+[v],\qquad
 [u+v]=[u]+[v]\ \text{if }uv=0,\qquad
 [s^*s]=[ss^*].$$ Positive domination implies Cuntz comparison; elements of $\operatorname{her}(v)_+$ are Cuntz below $v$. If $u\in\operatorname{Ideal}(v)_+$, then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there is $N<\infty$ with $[(u-\varepsilon)_+]\le N[v]$. Also $$[u]=\sup_{\varepsilon>0}[(u-\varepsilon)_+],
 \qquad [(u-\varepsilon)_+]\ll[u],$$ where $x\ll y$ means that any increasing sequence with supremum at least $y$ has a term dominating $x$. Spectral cuts are cofinal below $[u]$ for this relation. Projection classes are compact, and if a projection $p$ is Cuntz below $v$, then $\operatorname{her}(v)$ contains a projection Murray–von Neumann equivalent to $p$ (with matrix amplification when needed). These properties hold without simplicity or unitality; see <a id="pm-cite-45"></a>[[Rordam](#ref-Rordam), Section 4] and <a id="pm-cite-46"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Sections 2 and 4].

<a id="stmt-prelim-full-compressions"></a>
<div id="prelim:full-compressions" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Finite compressions of a full element). *If $v\in D_+$ is full and $D$ is unital, then there are $y_1,\ldots,y_N\in D$ such that $$\sum_{l=1}^N y_l^*vy_l=1_D.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-full-compressions"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-full-compressions</summary>

*Proof.* The ideal criterion above, applied to the projection $1_D$, gives $[1_D]\le N[v]$ for some $N$. A sufficiently accurate Cuntz approximation, compressed to the $1_D$ corner, gives $x_1,\ldots,x_N\in D$ with $\|1_D-\sum_lx_l^*vx_l\|<1$. The positive element $z=\sum_lx_l^*vx_l$ is invertible. Taking $y_l=x_lz^{-1/2}$ gives the identity. ◻

</details>

A dimension function on $W(D)$ is an additive order-preserving map $d:W(D)\to[0,\infty]$ with $d(0)=0$; no continuity is implicit. The semigroup is *almost unperforated* when $(m+1)x\le my$ implies $x\le y$, for $m\in\mathbb N$.

<a id="stmt-prelim-comparison"></a>
<div id="prelim:comparison" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Rørdam’s comparison criterion). *If $D\cong D\otimes\mathcal Z$, then $W(D)$ is almost unperforated. More generally, if $W(D)$ is almost unperforated, $u,v\in D_+$, $u\in\operatorname{Ideal}_D(v)$, and $$d([u])<d([v])\quad\text{for every dimension function $d$ with }d([v])=1,$$ then $u\precsim v$.*

</div>

These are <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[Rordam](#ref-Rordam), Theorem 4.5 and Corollary 4.7]. The second assertion uses abstract dimension functions, and requires neither exactness nor a trace-space hypothesis.

<a id="prelim:trace-cut"></a>
A lower semicontinuous extended trace on $D$ is an additive homogeneous map $\tau:D_+\to[0,\infty]$ satisfying $\tau(x^*x)=\tau(xx^*)$ and norm lower semicontinuity. We allow traces that are not densely finite. Their extensions to finite matrices use the unnormalized matrix trace, and their extensions to the stabilization are understood. For a positive contraction $z$, put $$d_\tau(z)=\lim_{l\to\infty}\tau(z^{1/l}).$$ Scaling a nonzero positive element to a contraction gives the same definition for arbitrary $z\ge0$. We use $\tau(z)\le d_\tau(z)$ for contractions and $$\label{prelim:trace-cut}
 d_\tau((z-\eta)_+)\le\eta^{-1}\tau(z)\qquad(\eta>0).$$

The next lemma follows from Elliott–Robert–Santiago’s comparison criterion <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Proposition 6.2] in the presence of a uniform rank gap. If $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ is almost unperforated, their criterion gives $x\le y$ for $x,y\in\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ from the ideal containment $x\le\sup_n ny$ and the strict inequalities $\lambda(x)<\lambda(y)$ for every functional $\lambda$ with $\lambda(y)=1$. A functional is an additive order-preserving map to $[0,\infty]$ that vanishes at zero and preserves increasing suprema. For exact algebras, the extended trace correspondence converts the rank gap into these inequalities, while the trace that is zero on an ideal and infinite outside it detects ideal containment. We give the direct regularization proof.

<a id="stmt-prelim-half-comparison"></a>
<div id="prelim:half-comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Comparison with a uniform rank gap). *Let $D$ be exact and $\mathcal Z$-stable. If $u,v\in D_+$ satisfy $$d_\tau(u)\le\tfrac12d_\tau(v)
 \quad\text{for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $D$},$$ then $u\precsim v$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-half-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-half-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Let $I=\operatorname{Ideal}_D(v)$. The map that is zero on $I_+$ and infinity on $D_+\setminus I$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace: additivity follows from heredity of $I$, the trace identity from $x^*x\in I\Longleftrightarrow xx^*\in I$, and lower semicontinuity from closedness of $I$. The assumed inequality for this trace forces $u\in I$.

Work in $D\otimes\mathcal K$, whose $W$-semigroup identifies with $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ and is almost unperforated. Fix a dimension function $d$ on this semigroup with $d([v])=1$. Its regularization is $$\overline d([z])=\sup_{\delta>0}d([(z-\delta)_+]).$$ By <a id="pm-cite-49"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Lemma 4.7] and the spectral-cut description of compact containment, $\overline d$ is a functional on $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$. The correspondence in <a id="pm-cite-50"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Proposition 4.2] realizes it as $d_\lambda$ for a lower semicontinuous extended $2$-quasitrace. Haagerup proved that finite-valued $2$-quasitraces on exact unital algebras are traces. Blanchard–Kirchberg extended this conclusion to lower semicontinuous $2$-quasitraces on exact algebras, allowing a possibly proper finite-domain ideal <a id="pm-cite-51"></a>[[ERS](#ref-ERS), Remarks 4.3 and 4.5]. Thus $\lambda$ is a trace. Restrict $\lambda$ to the original corner $D$. For each fixed $\eta>0$ the hypothesis gives $$\begin{split}
 d([(u-\eta)_+])
 &\le\overline d([u])=d_\lambda(u)
 \le\tfrac12d_\lambda(v)
 =\tfrac12\overline d([v])\\
 &\le\tfrac12<1=d([v]).
 \end{split}$$ The strict inequality is against $d([v])$, so it remains valid when $\overline d([v])=0$. Theorem <a href="#prelim:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:comparison">8</a> now compares every $(u-\eta)_+$ with $v$. Taking the supremum over cuts gives $u\precsim v$. Finally, comparisons between elements of the original corner can be witnessed in that corner, by compressing each witness on both sides by its multiplier projection. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prelim-matrix-traces"></a>
<div id="prelim:matrix-traces" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10** (Extended traces on finite matrices). *Let $A$ be unital, let $k\ge1$, and let $\lambda$ be a lower semicontinuous extended trace on $A\otimes M_k$. Set $\tau(a)=\lambda(a\otimes1_k)$ and $L_k=\mathrm{id}_A\otimes\operatorname{tr}_k$, with normalized matrix trace. Then $$\lambda(z)=\tau(L_k(z))\qquad(z\in(A\otimes M_k)_+).$$ No finiteness assumption on $\lambda$ is needed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-matrix-traces"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-matrix-traces</summary>

*Proof.* Write $e_{ij}=1_A\otimes e_{ij}$. Additivity and the trace identity give $$\lambda(z)
 =\sum_{i=1}^k\lambda(z^{1/2}e_{ii}z^{1/2})
 =\sum_{i=1}^k\lambda(e_{ii}ze_{ii}).$$ Unitary equivalence of the diagonal corners gives $\lambda(a\otimes e_{ii})=k^{-1}\tau(a)$ for $a\in A_+$. Summing proves the formula. Only finite sums and multiplication by positive scalars occur, so the argument also applies to infinite values. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Ultraproducts and bounded witnesses

Fix a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. For a sequence $(D_n)$ we use the norm ultraproduct $$\prod_\omega D_n
 =\prod_nD_n\big/\{(x_n):\lim_{n\to\omega}\|x_n\|=0\}.$$ For a constant sequence this is denoted $D_\omega$; the ordinary sequence algebra is $D_\infty=\ell^\infty(D)/c_0(D)$.

The following form of Kirchberg’s $\varepsilon$-test is recorded in <a id="pm-cite-52"></a>[[BBSTWW](#ref-BBSTWW), Lemma 1.10]; we include the diagonal proof.

<a id="stmt-prelim-epsilon-test"></a>
<div id="prelim:epsilon-test" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Kirchberg’s $\varepsilon$-test). *Let $X_n$ be nonempty sets and let $f_{j,n}:X_n\to[0,\infty)$ be given for $j,n\in\mathbb N$. Suppose that for every $m$ and $\delta>0$ there is $(x_n)\in\prod_nX_n$ with $\lim_{n\to\omega}f_{j,n}(x_n)<\delta$ for $1\le j\le m$. Then there is a single $(x_n)$ for which all these ultralimits are zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-epsilon-test"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-epsilon-test</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $(x_n^{(m)})$ solving the first $m$ conditions with ultralimits less than $1/(2m)$. Choose $A_m\in\omega$ on which the first $m$ errors of this choice are less than $1/m$, and set $E_m=A_1\cap\cdots\cap A_m$. These sets are decreasing and belong to $\omega$. At coordinate $n$, use $x_n^{(l(n))}$, where $l(n)=\max\{m\le n:n\in E_m\}$, using any element of $X_n$ if the set is empty. Then $l(n)\to_\omega\infty$, and every fixed error tends to zero. ◻

</details>

We apply this to countably many norm relations on contractions and on variables with fixed, separately prescribed norm bounds. Nuclear c.p.c. lifting permits the same procedure for maps out of a separable nuclear algebra; a countable dense set records linear relations, complete positivity and the needed order-zero relations. Countably many extra commutators or norms after a fixed contractive map can be included among the tests. The following elementary estimate supplies the bounds for Cuntz witnesses.

<a id="stmt-prelim-bounded-witness"></a>
<div id="prelim:bounded-witness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 12** (A spare spectral cut gives bounded witnesses). *Let $u,y\in D_+$ and $0\le a<b$. If $u\precsim(y-b)_+$, then for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $r\in D$ such that $$\|u-r^*(y-a)_+r\|<\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \|r\|^2\le\frac{\|u\|+\varepsilon}{b-a}.$$ Here $(y-0)_+=y$. In particular the bound is uniform in a family of contractions $u$ with a common gap $b-a>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prelim-bounded-witness"><summary>Proof of stmt-prelim-bounded-witness</summary>

*Proof.* Choose $s$ with $\|u-s^*(y-b)_+s\|<\varepsilon$ and put $$f(t)=\left(\frac{(t-b)_+}{(t-a)_+}\right)^{1/2},
 \qquad r=f(y)s,$$ where the ratio is zero when its denominator vanishes. This is a continuous function vanishing near zero, and $$f(y)(y-a)_+f(y)=(y-b)_+,
 \qquad f(t)^2\le(b-a)^{-1}(t-b)_+.$$ Consequently $$r^*(y-a)_+r=s^*(y-b)_+s,
 \qquad
 \|r\|^2\le(b-a)^{-1}\|s^*(y-b)_+s\|,$$ which proves the claim. If the original comparison is formulated in the stabilization, compression to the original corner gives $s\in D$. ◻

</details>

The choices $(a,b)=(c,2c)$ and $(a,b)=(0,c)$ will both be used. Together with Lemma <a href="#prelim:epsilon-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:epsilon-test">11</a>, the estimate permits coordinatewise comparisons with a fixed spare cut to pass to the norm ultraproduct. Coordinatewise Cuntz comparison without such control does not itself supply bounded representatives.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Domination and unitary equivalence

<a id="stmt-prelim-domination"></a>
<div id="prelim:domination" class="theorem">

**Theorem 13** (Gabe’s approximate domination theorem). *Let $D$ be exact and let $\Phi,\Psi:D\to E$ be nuclear completely positive maps, with $\Psi$ of order zero. Suppose $$\Phi(a)\in\operatorname{Ideal}_E(\Psi(a))\qquad(a\in D_+).$$ For every finite $\mathcal F\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there are $y_1,\ldots,y_N\in E$ such that $$\left\|\Phi(a)-\sum_{l=1}^Ny_l^*\Psi(a)y_l\right\|<\varepsilon
 \qquad(a\in\mathcal F).$$*

</div>

This is the implication from pointwise ideal containment to approximate domination in <a id="pm-cite-53"></a>[[Gabe](#ref-Gabe), Theorem 3.3], building on the ideal-separation method of <a id="pm-cite-54"></a>[[KR05](#ref-KR05), Proposition 4.2]. The approximation is in norm simultaneously on $\mathcal F$, and the codomain may be a nonexact norm ultraproduct. No bound on the individual $y_l$ is asserted.

<a id="stmt-prelim-unitary"></a>
<div id="prelim:unitary" class="lemma">

**Lemma 14** (Relative-commutant unitary equivalence). *Let $D_n$ be separable unital $\mathcal Z$-stable algebras, put $D_\omega=\prod_\omega D_n$, and let $A$ be separable and unital. For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\rho:A\to D_\omega$ set $$C=D_\omega\cap\rho(A)'\cap\{1-\rho(1)\}^{\perp}.$$ Assume that $C$ is full in $D_\omega$ and that every full hereditary subalgebra $E\subset C$ has the following property: whenever $x\in E$ admits totally full $e_l,e_r\in E_+$ with $e_lx=xe_r=0$, there is a full $s\in E$ satisfying $sx=xs=0$. If $h_0,h_1\in C_+$ are totally full contractions and $$f(h_0)\sim f(h_1)\quad\text{in }C
 \qquad\text{for every }f\in C_0((0,1])_+,$$ then $uh_1u^*=h_0$ for some unitary $u\in\widetilde C$.*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[BBSTWW](#ref-BBSTWW), Lemma 5.3], whose proof adapts the positive-element classification method of Robert–Santiago <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[RS](#ref-RS)]; $\sim$ here denotes Cuntz equivalence. We will verify the hereditary-subalgebra condition directly. After adjoining the ambient unit, the implementing unitary belongs to $D_\omega$ and commutes with $\rho(A)$. More explicitly, if $C$ already has a unit $p_C$ different from the ambient unit, replace a unitary $u\in C$ by $u+(1-p_C)$; otherwise use the canonical map from the unitization to $C+\mathbb C1_{D_\omega}$.

<a id="sec:model"></a>
### Matrix models with local trace control

<span id="model:section" label="model:section"></span>

Throughout this section, $A$ is a nonzero separable unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra; no absorption hypothesis is imposed. We construct a matrix model with trace estimates localized to finitely many ideals. The point is that a norm error inside a suitable hereditary corner becomes a trace error relative to the rank of the positive element generating the relevant ideal. For $k\geq 1$, write $$L_k=\mathrm{id}_A\otimes\mathrm{tr}_k\colon A\otimes M_k\longrightarrow A,
 \qquad
 L_k((z_{rs})_{r,s=1}^k)=\frac1k\sum_{r=1}^k z_{rr},$$ where $\mathrm{tr}_k$ is the normalized matrix trace. We may choose all matrix sizes to be powers of $2$; this convenient restriction costs nothing in the finite probability-rounding argument below.

Each test uses a positive contraction $a$ and a number $0<\gamma<1$. We keep the uncut element $a$ for the later comparison target, estimate the model error on the deeper cut $x=(a-\gamma)_+$, and use the shallower cut $b=(a-\gamma/2)_+$ to specify both the ideal and the rank scale for that estimate. The two cuts have distinct roles: $x$ lies in the hereditary subalgebra generated by $b$, whose rank $d_\tau(b)$ will control the trace error.

<a id="model:tests"></a>
Choose a countable dense subset $\mathcal D$ of the positive unit ball of $A$ which is closed under cuts $a\mapsto(a-r)_+$ for positive rational $r$. Such a set is obtained from any countable dense subset by adjoining its successive rational cuts. Enumerate all pairs $(a,\gamma)\in\mathcal D\times((0,1)\cap\mathbb Q)$ as $(a_j,\gamma_j)_{j\geq1}$, and set $$\label{model:tests}
 x_j=(a_j-\gamma_j)_+,
 \qquad b_j=(a_j-\gamma_j/2)_+,
 \qquad I_j=\operatorname{Ideal}(b_j).$$ In particular, $x_j\in\operatorname{her}(b_j)$ and $d_\tau(x_j)\leq d_\tau(b_j)$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$.

<a id="stmt-model-construction"></a>
<div id="model:construction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 15** (Matrix model). *There are finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of $2$, and completely positive contractive maps $$A\xrightarrow{\alpha_n}F_n
 \xrightarrow{\beta_n}A\otimes M_{k_n},
 \qquad \theta_n=\beta_n\alpha_n,$$ with the following properties.*

1.  *Each $\beta_n$ has order zero, and $$\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\|\longrightarrow0
      \quad(c,d\in A_+,\ cd=0).$$*

2.  *For every $a\in A$, $$\|L_{k_n}(\theta_n(a))-a\|\longrightarrow0.$$*

<a id="model:retained-norm"></a>
<a id="model:retained-trace"></a>
3.  *For every $n,j\geq1$ there is a positive contraction $w_{n,j}\in A\otimes M_{k_n}$, and there are nonnegative numbers $\eta_{n,j},\delta_{n,j}$ tending to zero as $n\to\infty$ for each fixed $j$, such that $$\begin{aligned}
      \|w_{n,j}-\theta_n(x_j)\|&\leq\eta_{n,j},
           \label{model:retained-norm}\\
      \tau(L_{k_n}(w_{n,j}))
         &\leq \tau(x_j)+\delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b_j)
           \label{model:retained-trace}
     
    \end{aligned}$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $A$ with $d_\tau(b_j)<\infty$.*

*The coefficients in <a href="#model:retained-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:retained-trace">[model:retained-trace]</a> are independent of $\tau$. One may take $\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$.*

</div>

We first establish the finite-stage construction. Its trace estimates will be obtained within hereditary subalgebras on which the relevant extended traces are bounded.

<a id="stmt-model-corner-traces"></a>
<div id="model:corner-traces" class="lemma">

<a id="model:corner-bound"></a>
<a id="model:corner-error"></a>
**Lemma 16** (Trace bounds in a hereditary subalgebra). *Let $P,b\in A_+$ be contractions, and suppose that $[P]\leq K[b]$ for a finite positive integer $K$. If $\tau$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace with $d_\tau(b)<\infty$, then its restriction to $D=\operatorname{her}(P)$ extends to a bounded positive linear functional satisfying $$\label{model:corner-bound}
 \|\tau|_D\|\leq d_\tau(P)\leq Kd_\tau(b).$$ Consequently, for $y,z\in D_+$, $$\label{model:corner-error}
 |\tau(y)-\tau(z)|
    \leq \|y-z\|K d_\tau(b).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-model-corner-traces"><summary>Proof of stmt-model-corner-traces</summary>

*Proof.* Choose an approximate unit $(e_l)$ for $D$ consisting of positive contractive continuous functions of $P$ which vanish at zero. Such elements satisfy $d_\tau(e_l)\leq d_\tau(P)$. For any positive contraction $z\in D$, the elements $e_lze_l$ converge to $z$ in norm, and $$\tau(e_lze_l)\leq\tau(e_l^2)\leq d_\tau(P).$$ Lower semicontinuity therefore gives $\tau(z)\leq d_\tau(P)$. Monotonicity and additivity of $d_\tau$ on Cuntz classes give the second inequality in <a href="#model:corner-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:corner-bound">[model:corner-bound]</a>. Thus $\tau$ is bounded on the positive unit ball of $D$ and extends to the asserted bounded positive functional. Its norm bound proves <a href="#model:corner-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:corner-error">[model:corner-error]</a>. This argument also applies when $d_\tau(b)=0$, in which case the restriction is identically zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-model-slicing"></a>
<div id="model:slicing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 17** (Finite ideal slicing). *Fix $m,N\geq1$, and let $\mathcal T=\{1,\ldots,N\}^m$. For every $t\in\mathcal T$ and $S\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}$, there are positive contractions $(P_{S,t,i})_{i\geq1}$ in $A$ with the following properties.*

1.  *For fixed $t,i$, the family $(P_{S,t,i})_S$ is orthogonal; in particular, $\sum_S P_{S,t,i}\leq1$.*

2.  *For every $a\in A$, $\|[P_{S,t,i},a]\|\to0$. If $1\leq j\leq m$ and $j\notin S$, then $$\|P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}x_jP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}\|\longrightarrow0.$$*

<a id="model:finite-rank-bound"></a>
3.  *If $j\in S$, then $P_{S,t,i}\in I_j$, and there is a finite positive integer $K_{S,t,i,j}$ such that $$\label{model:finite-rank-bound}
      [P_{S,t,i}]\leq K_{S,t,i,j}[b_j].$$*

<a id="model:slicing-approximation"></a>
4.  *For every $a\in A$, $$\label{model:slicing-approximation}
      \limsup_{i\to\infty}
      \left\|\frac1{N^m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\sum_S
         P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}aP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}-a\right\|
      \leq\frac mN\|a\|.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-model-slicing"><summary>Proof of stmt-model-slicing</summary>

*Proof.* Write $A_\infty=\ell^\infty(A)/c_0(A)$, identifying $A$ with its constant sequences. There are commuting positive contractions $u_1,\ldots,u_m\in A_\infty\cap A'$ such that $u_j$ is represented by positive contractions in $I_j$ and $u_jx_j=x_j$. Indeed, at coordinate $i$ choose successively an element of a quasicentral approximate unit of each $I_j$. Require its commutators with the first $i$ elements of a fixed dense subset of $A$ to have norm less than $1/i$, require that it act on $x_j$ within $1/i$, and require that it commute within $1/i$ with the elements already chosen at that coordinate. These are finitely many approximate-unit and quasicentrality requirements. They give exactly the stated relations on passing to $A_\infty$.

<a id="model:mean-slice-bound"></a>
Choose $N$ pairwise disjoint closed intervals contained in $(0,1)$. For the $r$th interval choose continuous functions $g_r^-,g_r^+\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]$ satisfying $$g_r^-g_r^+=0,\qquad
 0\leq g_r^-+g_r^+\leq1,$$ whose sum is one outside that interval. We choose $g_r^-$ equal to one below the interval and zero above it, and $g_r^+$ equal to zero below the interval and one above it. Within the interval the two functions can decrease and increase, respectively, meeting at zero. Set $h_r=g_r^-+g_r^+$. At every point of $[0,1]$ at most one interval can contribute a loss, so <a id="model:threshold-average"></a>$$\label{model:threshold-average}
 1-\frac1N\leq\frac1N\sum_{r=1}^N h_r\leq1.$$ For $t=(t_1,\ldots,t_m)\in\mathcal T$ define $$P_{S,t}=
  \prod_{j\in S}g_{t_j}^+(u_j)
  \prod_{j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\setminus S}g_{t_j}^-(u_j).$$ These are central positive contractions, and for fixed $t$ they are pairwise orthogonal as $S$ varies. Families for different values of $t$ may overlap and are combined by averaging. Joint functional calculus and independent averaging of the coordinates of $t$ give <a id="model:mean-slice"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 Q&=\frac1{N^m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\sum_S P_{S,t}
   =\prod_{j=1}^m\left(\frac1N\sum_{r=1}^N h_r(u_j)\right),
       \label{model:mean-slice}\\
 (1-1/N)^m1&\leq Q\leq1,
 \qquad \|1-Q\|\leq m/N.
       \label{model:mean-slice-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ If $j\in S$, then $P_{S,t}$ belongs to the image of $\ell^\infty(I_j)$ in $A_\infty$, since $g_{t_j}^+(0)=0$. If $1\leq j\leq m$ and $j\notin S$, then $g_{t_j}^-(u_j)x_j=g_{t_j}^-(1)x_j=0$, whence $P_{S,t}x_j=0$.

We construct the required orthogonal representatives directly, before imposing exact ideal membership. Start with positive contractive representatives of a finite orthogonal family, whose pairwise products tend to zero. To separate a first representative $a_i$ from a later representative $b_i$, choose $r_i>0$ tending to zero slowly enough that $\|a_ib_i\|/r_i\to0$. Let $f_i\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]$ be one on $[0,r_i/2]$ and zero on $[r_i,1]$, with $1-f_i(t)\leq2t/r_i$. Replace the first element by $(a_i-r_i)_+$ and every later one by $f_i(a_i)b_if_i(a_i)$. These replacements are orthogonal to the first cut, and $$\|b_i-f_i(a_i)b_if_i(a_i)\|
    \leq4\|a_ib_i\|/r_i\longrightarrow0.$$ Choose $r_i$ using the maximum of the finitely many relevant products. Repeating with the later elements preserves orthogonality already obtained and changes no quotient element. This gives, for every $t$, orthogonal positive contractive lifts $R_{S,t,i}$ of $P_{S,t}$.

For $j\in S$, the distance from $R_{S,t,i}$ to $I_j$ tends to zero. Let $\varepsilon_i>0$ tend to zero and dominate all these distances, over the finite collection of triples $(S,t,j)$ under consideration. The first cuts $$T_{S,t,i}=(R_{S,t,i}-2\varepsilon_i)_+$$ belong exactly to every $I_j$ with $j\in S$: their images in $A/I_j$ vanish by functional calculus. Choose another sequence $\sigma_i>0$ tending to zero, and put $$P_{S,t,i}=(T_{S,t,i}-\sigma_i)_+.$$ Because $T_{S,t,i}\in\operatorname{Ideal}(b_j)$, the usual ideal criterion for Cuntz comparison gives a finite integer $K_{S,t,i,j}$ for which <a href="#model:finite-rank-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-rank-bound">[model:finite-rank-bound]</a> holds. Here the strict positivity of the second cut is essential: every positive cut of an element in $\operatorname{Ideal}(b_j)$ is Cuntz below a finite direct sum of copies of $b_j$. Both cuts preserve orthogonality and change the representatives by at most $2\varepsilon_i+\sigma_i\to0$. Consequently they preserve all the quotient relations established above, while imposing all the ideal and finite-multiple conditions simultaneously.

Finally, centrality of $P_{S,t}$ gives $P_{S,t}^{1/2}aP_{S,t}^{1/2}=P_{S,t}a$ in $A_\infty$. Thus <a href="#model:mean-slice-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:mean-slice-bound">[model:mean-slice-bound]</a> implies <a href="#model:slicing-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:slicing-approximation">[model:slicing-approximation]</a>, and $P_{S,t}x_j=0$ proves the required vanishing of the omitted compressions. No bound on $K_{S,t,i,j}$ uniform in $i$ has been asserted or used. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-model-finite-stage"></a>
<div id="model:finite-stage" class="lemma">

<a id="model:finite-approx"></a>
<a id="model:finite-orth"></a>
<a id="model:finite-retained"></a>
<a id="model:finite-trace"></a>
**Lemma 18** (Finite-stage model). *Fix $m\geq1$, a finite subset $\mathcal F$ of the unit ball of $A$, a finite family $\mathcal O$ of pairs of orthogonal positive contractions in $A$, and $\varepsilon>0$. There are a finite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra $F$, a power $k$ of $2$, completely positive contractive maps $$A\xrightarrow{\alpha}F\xrightarrow{\beta}A\otimes M_k,
 \qquad\theta=\beta\alpha,$$ and positive contractions $w_1,\ldots,w_m\in A\otimes M_k$ such that $\beta$ has order zero and $$\begin{aligned}
 \|L_k(\theta(a))-a\|&<\varepsilon
       &&(a\in\mathcal F), \label{model:finite-approx}\\
 \|\alpha(c)\alpha(d)\|&<\varepsilon
       &&((c,d)\in\mathcal O), \label{model:finite-orth}\\
 \|w_j-\theta(x_j)\|&<\varepsilon
       &&(1\leq j\leq m), \label{model:finite-retained}\\
 \tau(L_k(w_j))&\leq\tau(x_j)+\varepsilon d_\tau(b_j)
       &&(1\leq j\leq m, d_\tau(b_j)<\infty).
       \label{model:finite-trace}
\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality holds for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ satisfying the indicated finite-rank condition.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-model-finite-stage"><summary>Proof of stmt-model-finite-stage</summary>

<a id="model:fixed-average"></a>
<a id="model:fixed-products"></a>
*Proof.* *Fixing the corners.* Choose $N$ so large that $m/N<\varepsilon/4$, and use Lemma <a href="#model:slicing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:slicing">17</a>. Write $$l_{S,t,i}(a)=P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}aP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}.$$ Asymptotic centrality implies, for $(c,d)\in\mathcal O$, $$\|l_{S,t,i}(c)l_{S,t,i}(d)\|
 =\|P_{S,t,i}^{1/2}cP_{S,t,i}dP_{S,t,i}^{1/2}\|
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Together with <a href="#model:slicing-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:slicing-approximation">[model:slicing-approximation]</a> and the omitted compression estimates, this permits us to fix one coordinate $i$ such that <a id="model:fixed-omitted"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \left\|\frac1{N^m}\sum_{t,S}l_{S,t,i}(a)-a\right\|
   &<\varepsilon/3 &&(a\in\mathcal F),
       \label{model:fixed-average}\\
 \|l_{S,t,i}(x_j)\|&<\varepsilon
       &&(1\leq j\leq m,\ j\notin S), \label{model:fixed-omitted}\\
 \|l_{S,t,i}(c)l_{S,t,i}(d)\|&<\varepsilon/2
       &&((c,d)\in\mathcal O). \label{model:fixed-products}
\end{aligned}$$ All these requirements range over finite sets. From now on this coordinate is fixed. For each occurrence indexed by $(S,t)$, suppress $i$ and write $$P=P_{S,t,i},\quad D_P=\operatorname{her}(P),\quad
 l_P(a)=P^{1/2}aP^{1/2},\quad K_{P,j}=K_{S,t,i,j}.$$ Every subscript $P$ below retains this $(S,t)$ occurrence index. Thus coincident positive elements, including zero pieces, still receive separate corner approximations and outcome labels. In particular, the finitely many constants $K_{P,j}$ have already been fixed before any approximation accuracy within $D_P$ is chosen.

*Approximating within the corners.* Each $D_P$ is separable and nuclear. The Brown–Carrión–White approximation theorem, Theorem <a href="#prelim:bcw" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:bcw">6</a>, supplies finite-dimensional approximations with asymptotically order-zero first maps and return maps which are finite convex combinations of completely positive contractive order-zero maps. For a fixed corner, take a sequence of these first maps $\zeta_r\colon D_P\to E_r$. The asymptotic product estimate <a href="#prelim:asymptotic-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prelim:asymptotic-product">[prelim:asymptotic-product]</a> gives <a id="model:quotient-product-bound"></a>$$\label{model:quotient-product-bound}
 \limsup_{r\to\infty}\|\zeta_r(y)\zeta_r(z)\|
   \leq\|yz\|\qquad(y,z\in D_P).$$ Thus sufficiently late first maps have product norm less than $\varepsilon$ on the finitely many pairs $(l_P(c),l_P(d))$ in <a href="#model:fixed-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:fixed-products">[model:fixed-products]</a>. This uses the approximation sequence for the fixed corner; it requires no uniformity in a varying hereditary subalgebra.

<a id="model:inner-tolerances"></a>
<a id="model:inner-approx"></a>
<a id="model:inner-products"></a>
Choose positive numbers $\xi_P$ such that $$\label{model:inner-tolerances}
 \max_P\xi_P<\varepsilon/3,
 \qquad
 \frac1{N^m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\sum_{S\ni j}
       K_{P_{S,t,i},j}\xi_{P_{S,t,i}}<\varepsilon/2
       \quad(1\leq j\leq m).$$ There are only finitely many constants in these requirements, so such choices are possible. Choose a corresponding approximation in each $D_P$, denoted $$D_P\xrightarrow{\zeta_P}E_P
   \xrightarrow{\varphi_P}D_P,
 \qquad
 \varphi_P=\sum_{r=1}^{h_P}\lambda_{P,r}\beta_{P,r},$$ where the $\lambda_{P,r}$ are nonnegative and sum to one, and each $\beta_{P,r}$ is completely positive contractive and order zero. All component return maps for this corner have the same domain $E_P$ and use the same first map $\zeta_P$. Arrange that $$\begin{aligned}
 \|\varphi_P\zeta_Pl_P(a)-l_P(a)\|&<\xi_P
       &&\bigl(a\in\mathcal F\cup\{x_1,\ldots,x_m\}\bigr),
       \label{model:inner-approx}\\
 \|\zeta_Pl_P(c)\zeta_Pl_P(d)\|&<\varepsilon
       &&((c,d)\in\mathcal O).
       \label{model:inner-products}
\end{aligned}$$ For a zero corner take $E_P=0$ and the zero maps, with a single return component of weight one.

*Individual outcomes and their retained parts.* Consider the finite probability space whose outcomes $\nu$ consist of a tuple $t\in\mathcal T$ and, for each subset $S$, a choice $r_S\in\{1,\ldots,h_{P_{S,t,i}}\}$. Give $t$ uniform probability and make the return-map choices independently with the probabilities $\lambda_{P,r}$. Write $\lambda_\nu$ for the resulting probability, and let $$E_t=\bigoplus_S E_{P_{S,t,i}},\qquad
 \alpha_t(a)=\bigoplus_S\zeta_{P_{S,t,i}}l_{P_{S,t,i}}(a),$$ $$\beta_\nu((z_S)_S)=
   \sum_S\beta_{P_{S,t,i},r_S}(z_S),
 \qquad T_\nu=\beta_\nu\alpha_t.$$ The map $\alpha_t$ is completely positive contractive because a finite direct sum has the maximum norm. The hereditary subalgebras $D_{P_{S,t,i}}$ are mutually orthogonal for the single tuple $t$ used by the outcome. Consequently $\beta_\nu$ is completely positive contractive and order zero: each summand has these properties, and products between distinct summand ranges vanish. Moreover, <a href="#model:inner-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:inner-products">[model:inner-products]</a> bounds the product defect of every $\alpha_t$ by $\varepsilon$, uniformly over the outcomes.

<a id="model:expected-map"></a>
<a id="model:expected-approximation"></a>
Independence of the return-map choices gives $$\label{model:expected-map}
 \sum_\nu\lambda_\nu T_\nu(a)
   =\frac1{N^m}\sum_{t\in\mathcal T}\sum_S
       \varphi_{P_{S,t,i}}\zeta_{P_{S,t,i}}l_{P_{S,t,i}}(a).$$ For each fixed $t$, the differences in <a href="#model:inner-approx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:inner-approx">[model:inner-approx]</a> lie in orthogonal hereditary subalgebras, so their sum has norm at most $\max_P\xi_P$. Equations <a href="#model:fixed-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:fixed-average">[model:fixed-average]</a>, <a href="#model:inner-tolerances" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:inner-tolerances">[model:inner-tolerances]</a>, and <a href="#model:expected-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:expected-map">[model:expected-map]</a> therefore imply $$\label{model:expected-approximation}
 \left\|\sum_\nu\lambda_\nu T_\nu(a)-a\right\|
    <2\varepsilon/3\qquad(a\in\mathcal F).$$

<a id="model:outcome-retained"></a>
<a id="model:before-averaging"></a>
For $1\leq j\leq m$, retain only the corners indexed by $S\ni j$: $$\label{model:outcome-retained}
 W_{\nu,j}=
    \sum_{S\ni j}\beta_{P_{S,t,i},r_S}
                \zeta_{P_{S,t,i}}l_{P_{S,t,i}}(x_j).$$ This is a positive contraction. Contractivity of each component and orthogonality of the omitted corners give, for every individual outcome, $$\label{model:before-averaging}
 \|T_\nu(x_j)-W_{\nu,j}\|
   \leq\max_{S\not\ni j}\|l_{P_{S,t,i}}(x_j)\|
   <\varepsilon.$$ The maximum over an empty family is taken to be zero. In particular, the estimate holds before taking any average.

<a id="model:expected-trace"></a>
<a id="model:individual-trace"></a>
*The uniform relative trace estimate.* Fix $j$ and a lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ with $d_\tau(b_j)<\infty$. In every retained corner, Lemma <a href="#model:corner-traces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:corner-traces">16</a> applies with the already fixed constant $K_{P,j}$. Thus <a href="#model:inner-approx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:inner-approx">[model:inner-approx]</a> implies $$\tau(\varphi_P\zeta_Pl_P(x_j))
    \leq\tau(l_P(x_j))+\xi_PK_{P,j}d_\tau(b_j)
    \qquad(j\in S).$$ All quantities in this inequality are finite. For each fixed $t$, traciality and $\sum_{S\ni j}P_{S,t,i}\leq1$ give $$\begin{aligned}
 \sum_{S\ni j}\tau(l_{P_{S,t,i}}(x_j))
 &=\tau\left(x_j^{1/2}
       \Bigl(\sum_{S\ni j}P_{S,t,i}\Bigr)x_j^{1/2}\right)\\
 &\leq\tau(x_j).
\end{aligned}$$ Taking expectations in <a href="#model:outcome-retained" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:outcome-retained">[model:outcome-retained]</a> and using <a href="#model:inner-tolerances" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:inner-tolerances">[model:inner-tolerances]</a> proves $$\label{model:expected-trace}
 \sum_\nu\lambda_\nu\tau(W_{\nu,j})
    \leq\tau(x_j)+(\varepsilon/2)d_\tau(b_j).$$ For later use there is also an individual bound $$\label{model:individual-trace}
 \tau(W_{\nu,j})\leq M_{\nu,j}d_\tau(b_j),
 \qquad M_{\nu,j}=\sum_{S\ni j}K_{P_{S,t,i},j}<\infty.$$ Every coefficient here was chosen independently of $\tau$. If $d_\tau(b_j)=0$, then each retained output has trace exactly zero, as does $x_j$. Hence the same inequalities hold in this case without an absolute additive error. No condition is being placed on traces for which $d_\tau(b_j)=\infty$.

<a id="model:rounding-errors"></a>
*Rounding the probabilities and forming the matrix model.* After all corner maps and the constants $M_{\nu,j}$ have been fixed, choose $k=2^s$ and nonnegative integers $n_\nu$ with $\sum_\nu n_\nu=k$ so that $\mu_\nu=n_\nu/k$ satisfies $$\label{model:rounding-errors}
 \sum_\nu|\mu_\nu-\lambda_\nu|<\varepsilon/3,
 \qquad
 \sum_\nu|\mu_\nu-\lambda_\nu|M_{\nu,j}
      <\varepsilon/2\quad(1\leq j\leq m).$$ Such choices exist for arbitrarily large $s$: round the finitely many numbers $k\lambda_\nu$ down to integers and distribute the remaining units among the outcomes. The resulting probabilities stay in the probability simplex, and their total absolute error tends to zero as $s\to\infty$. Contractivity of the $T_\nu$ shows that replacing $\lambda_\nu$ by $\mu_\nu$ changes the expected map on the unit ball by less than $\varepsilon/3$. Equations <a href="#model:individual-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:individual-trace">[model:individual-trace]</a> and <a href="#model:rounding-errors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:rounding-errors">[model:rounding-errors]</a> show that the expected retained trace changes in absolute value by at most $(\varepsilon/2)d_\tau(b_j)$.

List the outcomes with their multiplicities as $\nu_1,\ldots,\nu_k$, and write $t_l$ for the threshold tuple in $\nu_l$. Define $$F=\bigoplus_{l=1}^k E_{t_l},\qquad
 \alpha(a)=\bigoplus_{l=1}^k\alpha_{t_l}(a),\qquad
 \beta((z_l)_{l=1}^k)
    =\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\beta_{\nu_l}(z_l)\bigr)_{l=1}^k.$$ The map $\alpha$ is completely positive contractive, and $\beta$ is completely positive contractive and order zero, by the orthogonality of the matrix diagonal positions. We have $$\theta(a)=\operatorname{diag}(T_{\nu_l}(a))_{l=1}^k,
 \qquad
 L_k(\theta(a))=\sum_\nu\mu_\nu T_\nu(a).$$ Consequently <a href="#model:expected-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:expected-approximation">[model:expected-approximation]</a> and <a href="#model:rounding-errors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:rounding-errors">[model:rounding-errors]</a> give <a href="#model:finite-approx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-approx">[model:finite-approx]</a>, and the maximum norm in the direct sum gives <a href="#model:finite-orth" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-orth">[model:finite-orth]</a>. Finally, put $$w_j=\operatorname{diag}(W_{\nu_l,j})_{l=1}^k.$$ These are positive contractions, and the estimate for individual outcomes in <a href="#model:before-averaging" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:before-averaging">[model:before-averaging]</a> proves <a href="#model:finite-retained" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-retained">[model:finite-retained]</a>. Since $L_k(w_j)=\sum_\nu\mu_\nu W_{\nu,j}$, <a href="#model:expected-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:expected-trace">[model:expected-trace]</a> and the trace part of the rounding estimate prove <a href="#model:finite-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-trace">[model:finite-trace]</a>. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-model-finite-stage-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-model-finite-stage</summary>

*Proof of Lemma <a href="#model:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:construction">15</a>.* Fix a countable dense subset $(c_l)_{l\geq1}$ of the unit ball of $A$. The space $$\{(c,d)\in A_+\times A_+:
           \|c\|,\|d\|\leq1,\ cd=0\}$$ is a separable metric space, so choose a countable dense family $(c'_l,d'_l)_{l\geq1}$ in this space. At stage $n$, apply Lemma <a href="#model:finite-stage" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:finite-stage">18</a> to the first $n$ tests, $\mathcal F_n=\{c_1,\ldots,c_n\}$, $\mathcal O_n=\{(c'_1,d'_1),\ldots,(c'_n,d'_n)\}$, and $\varepsilon=2^{-n}$. Denote the resulting maps by $\alpha_n$ and $\beta_n$, and the retained elements by $w_{n,j}$ for $j\leq n$.

Contractivity extends <a href="#model:finite-approx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:finite-approx">[model:finite-approx]</a> from the dense family to point-norm convergence on $A$. Likewise, if $c,d$ are orthogonal positive contractions and $(c',d')$ is one of the chosen orthogonal pairs, then $$\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\|
 \leq\|c-c'\|+\|d-d'\|
       +\|\alpha_n(c')\alpha_n(d')\|.$$ First taking the limit superior in $n$, then approximating $(c,d)$ by such pairs, proves asymptotic preservation of orthogonality. Scaling proves the assertion for arbitrary orthogonal positive elements.

For $j>n$ set $w_{n,j}=0$. Take $\delta_{n,j}=2^{-n}$ for all $n,j$, and set $$\eta_{n,j}=
 \begin{cases}
  2^{-n},&j\leq n,\\
  1,&j>n.
 \end{cases}$$ Equations <a href="#model:retained-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:retained-norm">[model:retained-norm]</a> and <a href="#model:retained-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:retained-trace">[model:retained-trace]</a> now hold for every $n,j$, and both error sequences tend to zero for each fixed $j$. At each stage the trace inequality holds simultaneously for all the indicated extended traces. Thus the diagonalization involves only the countable lists of elements, tests, and orthogonal pairs; it requires neither a countable family of traces nor a restriction on the ideal lattice of $A$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-model-composite-order-zero"></a>
<div id="model:composite-order-zero" class="remark">

*Remark 19*. The composites $\theta_n$ also asymptotically preserve orthogonality. Indeed, the order-zero product estimate gives $$\|\theta_n(c)\theta_n(d)\|
 \leq\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\|
 \qquad(c,d\in A_+).$$ Consequently they induce a c.p.c. order-zero map into $\prod_n(A\otimes M_{k_n})/\bigoplus_n(A\otimes M_{k_n})$, and into the corresponding norm ultraproduct. The same conclusion holds after composing the $\beta_n$ with any c.p.c. order-zero maps into a fixed target algebra: the displayed product estimate remains valid for each composite return map.

</div>

<a id="sec:traces"></a>
### The common part and localized trace comparison

The matrix model will now be placed in a Jiang–Su tensor factor by an order-zero map. Its expectation is still asymptotically the identity. This produces a common part of the model and the original inclusion. The remaining part is small in a sense that can be compared uniformly over all lower semicontinuous extended traces, including traces that are infinite away from an ideal.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Order-zero maps into the Jiang–Su algebra

Write $\tau_{\mathcal Z}$ for the unique tracial state of $\mathcal Z$. We first construct order-zero matrix maps and positive contractions with arbitrarily many orthogonal copies.

<a id="stmt-supplies-order-zero"></a>
<div id="supplies:order-zero" class="lemma">

**Lemma 20**. *For every integer $k\geq 1$ and every $\xi>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\iota\colon M_k\to\mathcal Z$ such that $$\tau_{\mathcal Z}(\iota(1))>1-\xi,
 \qquad \|\iota(e_{11})\|=1.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-supplies-order-zero"><summary>Proof of stmt-supplies-order-zero</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a multiple $d\geq2$ of $k$ with $(d+1)^{-1}<\xi$, and fix a unital embedding $\mu\colon M_k\to M_d$. Put $p=e_{d+1,d+1}\in M_{d+1}$. In $M_d\otimes M_{d+1}$, the complement of $1\otimes p$ is the tensor product of two $d$-dimensional spaces. Let $V$ be the flip on this complement, extended by the identity on the range of $1\otimes p$. The unitary group of each finite matrix block is path connected. There is therefore a continuous unitary path $U(t)$, $0\leq t\leq1$, from $1$ to $V$, all of whose members commute with $1\otimes p$.

Define $$\iota_0(x)(t)
   =U(t)\bigl(\mu(x)\otimes(1-tp)\bigr)U(t)^*.$$ This is a c.p.c. order-zero map into $C([0,1],M_d\otimes M_{d+1})$. Its endpoint values are $$\iota_0(x)(0)=\mu(x)\otimes1,
 \qquad
 \iota_0(x)(1)=1\otimes\operatorname{diag}(\mu(x),0).$$ Consequently its range lies in the prime dimension-drop algebra $$I_{d,d+1}
 =\{f\in C([0,1],M_d\otimes M_{d+1}):
 f(0)\in M_d\otimes1,\ f(1)\in1\otimes M_{d+1}\}.$$ The commutation of $U(t)$ with $1\otimes p$ gives the exact defect $$1-\iota_0(1)=\bigl(t\mapsto t(1\otimes p)\bigr).$$ For $1\leq r\leq d+1$, the functions $$y_r(t)=\sqrt t\,(1\otimes e_{r,d+1})$$ belong to $I_{d,d+1}$ and satisfy $$y_r^*y_r=1-\iota_0(1),
 \qquad
 \sum_{r=1}^{d+1}y_ry_r^*=(t\mapsto t1)\leq1.$$ Thus every tracial state $\sigma$ on $I_{d,d+1}$ satisfies $$(d+1)\sigma(1-\iota_0(1))
   =\sigma\left(\sum_{r=1}^{d+1}y_ry_r^*\right)\leq1.$$ The prime dimension-drop algebra $I_{d,d+1}$ embeds unitally into $\mathcal Z$ by <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[Rordam](#ref-Rordam), Theorem 2.1(i)]. Composing such an embedding with $\iota_0$ gives the trace estimate. Finally, $\|\iota_0(e_{11})(0)\|=1$, so isometry of the embedding gives the asserted norm. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-supplies-copies"></a>
<div id="supplies:copies" class="lemma">

<a id="supplies:copy-relations"></a>
**Lemma 21**. *For every integer $m\geq2$ there are a positive contraction $D_m\in\mathcal Z$ of norm one, contractions $v_{m,1},\ldots,v_{m,m}\in\mathcal Z$, and a finite positive integer $K_m$ such that $$\label{supplies:copy-relations}
 v_{m,r}^*v_{m,s}=\delta_{rs}D_m,
 \qquad
 1=\sum_{l=1}^{K_m}y_{m,l}^*(D_m-1/2)_+y_{m,l}$$ for suitable $y_{m,l}\in\mathcal Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-supplies-copies"><summary>Proof of stmt-supplies-copies</summary>

*Proof.* Take $\iota\colon M_m\to\mathcal Z$ from Lemma <a href="#supplies:order-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supplies:order-zero">20</a>, and set $$D_m=\iota(e_{11}),\qquad v_{m,r}=\iota^{1/2}(e_{r1}).$$ Order-zero functional calculus and the product identity <a href="#prelim:order-zero" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prelim:order-zero">[prelim:order-zero]</a> give the first identity in <a href="#supplies:copy-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supplies:copy-relations">[supplies:copy-relations]</a>. The element $(D_m-1/2)_+$ is nonzero, hence full in the simple unital algebra $\mathcal Z$. Lemma <a href="#prelim:full-compressions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:full-compressions">7</a> supplies the second identity. ◻

</details>

We fix these choices once and for all. No uniform bound on $K_m$ is required.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The model and its common part

Throughout this section, $A$ is a nonzero separable unital nuclear $C^*$-algebra. Set <a id="setup:ambient"></a>$$\label{setup:ambient}
 R=A\otimes\mathcal Z_1\otimes\mathcal Z_2\otimes\mathcal Z_3,
 \qquad B=R_\omega,
 \qquad B_1=(A\otimes\mathcal Z_1)_\omega\subset B.$$ The first Jiang–Su factor carries the model and its common part; the second supplies positive contractions with arbitrarily many orthogonal copies. The constant third factor is reserved for the complementary weights in the unitary-equivalence argument. The tensor-factor inclusions are unital. Write $\pi\colon A\to B_1\subset B$ for the constant first-factor inclusion. Nuclearity and strong self-absorption of $\mathcal Z$ <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[TW](#ref-TW), Examples 1.14] imply that $R$ is nuclear, exact, and $\mathcal Z$-stable.

<a id="setup:model-traces"></a>
Use the tests $a_j,\gamma_j,x_j,b_j$ from <a href="#model:tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:tests">[model:tests]</a>. The matrix-model construction, Lemma <a href="#model:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="model:construction">15</a>, gives finite-dimensional algebras $F_n$, powers $k_n$ of two, and c.p.c. maps $$A\xrightarrow{\alpha_n}F_n
   \xrightarrow{\beta_n}A\otimes M_{k_n},$$ where $\beta_n$ is order zero, $\alpha_n$ is asymptotically order zero, and $L_{k_n}\beta_n\alpha_n(a)\to a$ for every $a\in A$. Denote the lemma’s retained positive contractions by $W_{n,j}\in A\otimes M_{k_n}$. For each fixed $j$ they satisfy <a id="setup:model-retained"></a>$$\label{setup:model-retained}
 \|W_{n,j}-\beta_n\alpha_n(x_j)\|\longrightarrow0$$ and $$\label{setup:model-traces}
 \tau(L_{k_n}(W_{n,j}))
   \leq\tau(x_j)+\delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b_j)
 \qquad\bigl(d_\tau(b_j)<\infty\bigr).$$ Here $\tau$ ranges over all lower semicontinuous extended traces on $A$, and the nonnegative constants $\delta_{n,j}$ are independent of $\tau$ and tend to zero for fixed $j$.

Choose maps $\iota_n\colon M_{k_n}\to\mathcal Z_1$ from Lemma <a href="#supplies:order-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supplies:order-zero">20</a> with $s_n:=\tau_{\mathcal Z}(\iota_n(1))\to1$. Define <a id="setup:transformed-model"></a>$$\label{setup:transformed-model}
 \begin{aligned}
 T_n&=\mathrm{id}_A\otimes\iota_n,&
 \beta'_n&=T_n\beta_n,\\
 \theta_n&=\beta'_n\alpha_n,&
 w_{n,j}&=T_n(W_{n,j}).
 \end{aligned}$$ These maps and elements take values in $A\otimes\mathcal Z_1$, which we regard as a subalgebra of $R$.

The order-zero product identity for $\iota_n$ tensors with $\mathrm{id}_A$, first on elementary tensors and then by continuity. Thus $T_n$ and $\beta'_n$ are c.p.c. order-zero maps. Put $$L=\mathrm{id}_A\otimes\tau_{\mathcal Z}\colon A\otimes\mathcal Z_1\longrightarrow A.$$ The functional $\tau_{\mathcal Z}\circ\iota_n$ is a positive trace on $M_{k_n}$. Indeed, the square-root order-zero map satisfies $$\iota_n^{1/2}(z)^*\iota_n^{1/2}(z)=\iota_n(z^*z),
 \qquad
 \iota_n^{1/2}(z)\iota_n^{1/2}(z)^*=\iota_n(zz^*).$$ Its mass is $s_n$, so $$LT_n=s_nL_{k_n}.$$ It follows that $L\theta_n(a)\to a$ for every $a\in A$. The product estimate for an order-zero map also gives $$\|\theta_n(c)\theta_n(d)\|
 \leq\|\alpha_n(c)\alpha_n(d)\|\longrightarrow0
 \qquad(c,d\in A_+,\ cd=0).$$ Consequently the maps $\theta_n$ induce a c.p.c. order-zero map <a id="setup:theta"></a>$$\label{setup:theta}
 \theta\colon A\longrightarrow B_1,
 \qquad \theta(a)=(\theta_n(a))_\omega,
 \qquad (w_{n,j})_\omega=\theta(x_j).$$ The same letter $L$ will denote the induced unital completely positive map $B_1\to A_\omega$. With the constant inclusion of $A$ into $A_\omega$ understood, we have $L\theta=L\pi=\mathrm{id}_A$.

<a id="stmt-setup-common"></a>
<div id="setup:common" class="lemma">

<a id="setup:common-equations"></a>
**Lemma 22** (The common part). *There is a positive contraction $e\in B_1$ such that, with $q=1-e$, $$\label{setup:common-equations}
 L(e)=0,\qquad
 [e,\pi(a)]=[e,\theta(a)]=0,\qquad
 q\theta(a)=q\pi(a)
 \quad(a\in A).$$ In particular, if $h=\theta(1)$, then $qh=q$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-setup-common"><summary>Proof of stmt-setup-common</summary>

*Proof.* Both ranges $\pi(A)$ and $\theta(A)$ belong to the multiplicative domain of $L$. For $\theta$, the two Schwarz inequalities give $$a^*a=L(\theta(a))^*L(\theta(a))
 \leq L(\theta(a)^*\theta(a))
 \leq L(\theta(a^*a))=a^*a.$$ The analogous equalities hold with $a$ and $a^*$ interchanged. The assertion for $\pi$ follows in the same way, or directly from its multiplicativity.

Let $D_0=C^*(\pi(A),\theta(A))\subset B_1$. This is a separable unital algebra, $L|_{D_0}$ is a homomorphism, and $$J=\ker(L|_{D_0})$$ is an ideal containing $\theta(a)-\pi(a)$ for every $a\in A$. Choose a positive contractive quasicentral approximate unit $(f_l)$ for $J$ in $D_0$. Then $L(f_l)=0$, the commutators of $f_l$ with both ranges tend to zero, and $$(1-f_l)(\theta(a)-\pi(a))\longrightarrow0
 \qquad(a\in A).$$ Lift the $f_l$ to positive contractive sequences in $A\otimes\mathcal Z_1$. Apply the countable diagonalization of Lemma <a href="#prelim:epsilon-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:epsilon-test">11</a> to the preceding equations on dense countable subsets, together with the coordinate test $\|L(f_{l,n})\|$. The latter tends to zero along $\omega$ for each fixed $l$. The resulting positive contraction $e\in B_1$ satisfies <a href="#setup:common-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:common-equations">[setup:common-equations]</a>. Taking $a=1$ gives $qh=q$. ◻

</details>

Fix positive contractive representatives $e_n\in A\otimes\mathcal Z_1$ of $e$, and write <a id="setup:representatives"></a>$$\label{setup:representatives}
 q_n=1-e_n,\qquad \ell_n=\|L(e_n)\|.$$ Thus $\ell_n\to_\omega0$. Exact vanishing of $L(e_n)$ at any coordinate is neither asserted nor needed.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### Rank estimates on a prescribed ideal

Fix $j$ temporarily, and abbreviate $$a=a_j,\quad \gamma=\gamma_j,\quad
 x=x_j,\quad b=b_j,\quad w_n=w_{n,j}.$$ Inside $A\otimes\mathcal Z_1\subset R$, set <a id="trace:coordinate-positives"></a>$$\label{trace:coordinate-positives}
 \begin{split}
 u_{n,0}&=x^{1/2}e_nx^{1/2},\\
 u_{n,1}&=w_n^{1/2}e_nw_n^{1/2},\\
 z_n&=a^{1/2}(q_n-1/2)_+a^{1/2}.
 \end{split}$$ These are positive contractions.

<a id="stmt-trace-rank"></a>
<div id="trace:rank" class="lemma">

<a id="trace:small-rank"></a>
<a id="trace:large-rank"></a>
**Lemma 23** (Localized rank estimates). *For each $j$ there are nonnegative numbers $\varepsilon_{n,j}\to_\omega0$, independent of the trace, with the following properties. Let $\lambda$ be any lower semicontinuous extended trace on $R$, and let $\tau=\lambda|_A$. If $d_\tau(b)<\infty$, then, for $i=0,1$ and $\eta>0$, $$\label{trace:small-rank}
 d_\lambda((u_{n,i}-\eta)_+)
   \leq\eta^{-1}\varepsilon_{n,j}d_\tau(b).$$ If $\ell_n\leq1/4$, then, with no finiteness assumption, $$\label{trace:large-rank}
 d_\lambda((z_n-\gamma/16)_+)
   \geq(\gamma/16)d_\tau(b).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-trace-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-trace-rank</summary>

<a id="trace:localized-expectation"></a>
<a id="trace:first-small-part"></a>
*Proof.* We first work under the hypothesis $d_\tau(b)<\infty$. On the hereditary subalgebra $\operatorname{her}_A(b)\otimes\mathcal Z_1$ one has $$\label{trace:localized-expectation}
 \lambda(y)=\tau(L(y))\qquad(y\geq0).$$ To justify this formula, every positive contraction in $\operatorname{her}_R(b\otimes1)$ has $\lambda$-value at most $d_\lambda(b\otimes1)=d_\tau(b)$. The restriction of $\lambda$ to $\operatorname{her}_A(b)\otimes\mathcal Z_1$ therefore extends to a bounded trace; the same holds for $\tau$ on $\operatorname{her}_A(b)$. For a positive $s\in\operatorname{her}_A(b)$, the functional $$v\longmapsto\lambda(s\otimes v)$$ is a bounded positive trace on $\mathcal Z_1$, of mass $\tau(s)$. Its trace identity follows by applying the trace identity for $\lambda$ to $s^{1/2}\otimes z$. Uniqueness of the tracial state of $\mathcal Z$ gives $\lambda(s\otimes v)=\tau(s)\tau_{\mathcal Z}(v)$. Linearity and density prove <a href="#trace:localized-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:localized-expectation">[trace:localized-expectation]</a>. In particular, $$\label{trace:first-small-part}
 \lambda(u_{n,0})
 =\tau(x^{1/2}L(e_n)x^{1/2})
 \leq\ell_n\tau(x)
 \leq\ell_n d_\tau(b).$$

<a id="trace:retained-finite"></a>
There is a separate estimate for $w_n$, which need not lie in that hereditary subalgebra. Define $$\rho_n=\lambda\circ T_n,
 \qquad
 \sigma_n(s)=\rho_n(s\otimes1)\quad(s\in A_+).$$ Order-zero square-root calculus shows that $\rho_n$ is an extended trace; its lower semicontinuity follows from continuity of $T_n$. Furthermore, $$\sigma_n(s)
 =\lambda(s\otimes\iota_n(1))\leq\lambda(s\otimes1)=\tau(s).$$ Lemma <a href="#prelim:matrix-traces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:matrix-traces">10</a>, applied to $\rho_n$, gives <a id="trace:matrix-formula"></a>$$\label{trace:matrix-formula}
 \rho_n(Y)=\sigma_n(L_{k_n}(Y))
 \qquad(Y\in(A\otimes M_{k_n})_+).$$ This identity includes infinite values. It follows from <a href="#setup:model-traces" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:model-traces">[setup:model-traces]</a> that $$\label{trace:retained-finite}
 \lambda(w_n)
 \leq\tau(L_{k_n}(W_{n,j}))
 \leq\tau(x)+\delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b)<\infty.$$

<a id="trace:localized-norm-error"></a>
Put $$S_n=w_n^{1/2}q_nw_n^{1/2},\qquad
 S'_n=x^{1/2}q_nx^{1/2},\qquad
 \Delta_{n,j}=\|S_n-S'_n\|.$$ The common-part identity implies $\Delta_{n,j}\to_\omega0$: in the quotient, the two expressions are respectively $q\theta(x)$ and $q\pi(x)$. Compressing $S'_n\leq S_n+\Delta_{n,j}1$ by $x^{1/(2r)}$ gives $$\lambda(x^{1/(2r)}S'_nx^{1/(2r)})
 \leq\lambda(S_n)+\Delta_{n,j}\tau(x^{1/r})
 \leq\lambda(S_n)+\Delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b).$$ Here the compression of $S_n$ has trace at most $\lambda(S_n)$, by traciality and contractivity of $x^{1/(2r)}$. The compressed $S'_n$ converges in norm to $S'_n$ as $r\to\infty$. Lower semicontinuity yields $$\label{trace:localized-norm-error}
 \lambda(S'_n)\leq\lambda(S_n)+\Delta_{n,j}d_\tau(b).$$ All of $\lambda(w_n)$, $\lambda(S_n)$, $\lambda(S'_n)$, and $\tau(x)$ are finite in the case under consideration. We may therefore subtract these trace values. Since $x=S'_n+u_{n,0}$ and $w_n=S_n+u_{n,1}$, <a href="#trace:retained-finite" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:retained-finite">[trace:retained-finite]</a> and <a href="#trace:localized-norm-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:localized-norm-error">[trace:localized-norm-error]</a> give $$\lambda(u_{n,1})
 \leq\lambda(u_{n,0})
       +(\delta_{n,j}+\Delta_{n,j})d_\tau(b).$$ Together with <a href="#trace:first-small-part" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:first-small-part">[trace:first-small-part]</a>, this proves, for both $i=0,1$, $$\lambda(u_{n,i})
 \leq(\delta_{n,j}+\Delta_{n,j}+\ell_n)d_\tau(b).$$ Set <a id="trace:error-coefficient"></a>$$\label{trace:error-coefficient}
 \varepsilon_{n,j}=\delta_{n,j}+\Delta_{n,j}+\ell_n.$$ The trace-cut estimate <a href="#prelim:trace-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prelim:trace-cut">[prelim:trace-cut]</a> proves <a href="#trace:small-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:small-rank">[trace:small-rank]</a>. In particular, when $d_\tau(b)=0$ the right side is exactly zero, not an absolute error term.

<a id="trace:expectation-lower"></a>
We now prove <a href="#trace:large-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:large-rank">[trace:large-rank]</a>. Suppose $\ell_n\leq1/4$ and write $$t_n=(z_n-\gamma/16)_+.$$ Because $(q_n-1/2)_+\geq1/2-e_n$ and $t_n\geq z_n-(\gamma/16)1$, we have $$\label{trace:expectation-lower}
 L(t_n)\geq(1/2-\ell_n)a-(\gamma/16)1.$$ On the support of $b$, the element $a$ is at least $\gamma/2$. Consequently compression by $b^{1/(2r)}$ gives <a id="trace:compressed-lower"></a>$$\label{trace:compressed-lower}
 b^{1/(2r)}L(t_n)b^{1/(2r)}
 \geq(\gamma/16)b^{1/r}.$$ If $d_\tau(b)<\infty$, the compression $b^{1/(2r)}t_nb^{1/(2r)}$ belongs to $\operatorname{her}_A(b)\otimes\mathcal Z_1$, so <a href="#trace:localized-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:localized-expectation">[trace:localized-expectation]</a> and traciality imply $$\lambda(t_n)
 \geq\tau(b^{1/(2r)}L(t_n)b^{1/(2r)})
 \geq(\gamma/16)\tau(b^{1/r}).$$ Let $r\to\infty$ and use $d_\lambda(t_n)\geq\lambda(t_n)$, since $t_n$ is a contraction. This proves <a href="#trace:large-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:large-rank">[trace:large-rank]</a> in the finite-rank case, including rank zero.

<a id="trace:expectation-ideal"></a>
For the infinite-rank case we use ideals, without appealing to <a href="#trace:localized-expectation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:localized-expectation">[trace:localized-expectation]</a>. First, $$\label{trace:expectation-ideal}
 L(t_n)\otimes1\in\operatorname{Ideal}_R(t_n).$$ To see this, apply the domination theorem, Theorem <a href="#prelim:domination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:domination">13</a>, on $\mathcal Z_1$ to $v\mapsto\tau_{\mathcal Z}(v)1$ and the identity map. The source is exact, the maps are nuclear, the identity has order zero, and the required pointwise ideal containment follows from simplicity of $\mathcal Z$. There are therefore completely positive maps of the form $$V_l(v)=\sum_s z_{l,s}^*vz_{l,s}$$ converging pointwise in norm to $\tau_{\mathcal Z}(v)1$. Including $1$ in the approximation tests allows us to require $\|V_l\|\leq2$. Tensoring with $\mathrm{id}_A$ then preserves pointwise norm convergence: it holds first on elementary tensors, and the uniform norm bound extends it to their completion. Each resulting approximation to $L(t_n)\otimes1$ is a sum of compressions of $t_n$ in $R$, proving <a href="#trace:expectation-ideal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:expectation-ideal">[trace:expectation-ideal]</a>.

Let $I=\operatorname{Ideal}_R(t_n)$. In $R/I$, equation <a href="#trace:expectation-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:expectation-lower">[trace:expectation-lower]</a> and <a href="#trace:expectation-ideal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:expectation-ideal">[trace:expectation-ideal]</a> give $$0\geq(1/2-\ell_n)(a+I)-(\gamma/16)(1+I).$$ Thus $\|a+I\|\leq\gamma/4$, and $(a-\gamma/4)_+\in I$. Taking one more positive cut gives $$b=\bigl((a-\gamma/4)_+-\gamma/4\bigr)_+,
 \qquad [b]\leq M[t_n]$$ for some finite positive integer $M$. If $d_\tau(b)=\infty$, then $d_\lambda(b)=\infty$, so this comparison forces $d_\lambda(t_n)=\infty$. This finishes the proof in all cases. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Slowly chosen corners and bounded comparison witnesses

We let the number of available orthogonal copies in the second Jiang–Su factor grow slowly with the coordinate, so that the localized comparisons remain valid.

<a id="stmt-trace-comparison"></a>
<div id="trace:comparison" class="proposition">

<a id="trace:small-part-comparison"></a>
**Proposition 24** (Localized comparison). *There is a sequence $m(n)\geq2$ with $m(n)\to_\omega\infty$ such that, on putting $$P_n=D_{m(n)}\in\mathcal Z_2,\qquad P=(P_n)_\omega\in B,
 \qquad
 \Psi(a)=P(q-1/2)_+\pi(a),$$ the map $\Psi\colon A\to B$ is c.p.c. order zero and, for every $j\geq1$, $$\label{trace:small-part-comparison}
 e\pi(x_j),\ e\theta(x_j)
 \precsim (\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+
 \quad\text{in }B,
 \qquad c_j=\gamma_j/64.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-trace-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-trace-comparison</summary>

<a id="trace:separate-leg-comparison"></a>
*Proof.* Temporarily fix $m\geq2$ and $j$, and keep the abbreviations from the preceding proof. The copy of $D_m$ is now in $\mathcal Z_2$, separate from $z_n$ and $t_n=(z_n-\gamma/16)_+$. The compression identity in <a href="#supplies:copy-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supplies:copy-relations">[supplies:copy-relations]</a> gives $$\label{trace:separate-leg-comparison}
 [t_n]\leq K_m[t_n(D_m-1/2)_+]
       \leq K_m[(z_nD_m-\gamma/32)_+]
 \quad\text{in }W(R).$$ For the second inequality, use joint functional calculus and the scalar inequality $$(s-\gamma/16)_+(t-1/2)_+
      \leq(st-\gamma/32)_+\qquad(0\leq s,t\leq1).$$ Where the left side is positive, the difference between the uncut expression on the right and the left side is $s/2+(\gamma/16)t-\gamma/16\geq0$; elsewhere the assertion is immediate.

<a id="trace:slow-requirement"></a>
Enumerate the countable set of pairs $(j,\eta)$ with $j\geq1$ and $\eta\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$. For each $m\geq2$ let $\Omega_m$ be an $\omega$-large set on which $\ell_n\leq1/4$ and $$\label{trace:slow-requirement}
 \eta^{-1}\varepsilon_{n,j}
       \leq\frac{\gamma_j}{32K_m}$$ for all the first $m$ pairs. Such a set exists because the finitely many error coefficients tend to zero along $\omega$. Define $$E_m=\{n:n\geq m\}\cap\bigcap_{r=2}^m\Omega_r.$$ The sets $E_m$ are decreasing and $\omega$-large. If $n$ belongs to at least one $E_m$, let $m(n)$ be the largest such $m$; otherwise put $m(n)=2$. The largest value exists since membership in $E_m$ requires $m\leq n$. This construction gives $m(n)\to_\omega\infty$ and ensures that, for every fixed pair $(j,\eta)$, condition <a href="#trace:slow-requirement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:slow-requirement">[trace:slow-requirement]</a> with $m=m(n)$ holds on an $\omega$-large set.

<a id="trace:uniform-gap"></a>
Set $P_n=D_{m(n)}$. For each fixed $(j,\eta)$, on the preceding $\omega$-large set and for every lower semicontinuous extended trace $\lambda$ on $R$, we claim $$\label{trace:uniform-gap}
 d_\lambda((u_{n,i}-\eta)_+)
 \leq\frac12 d_\lambda((z_nP_n-\gamma_j/32)_+)
 \qquad(i=0,1).$$ When $d_\tau(b_j)<\infty$, combine <a href="#trace:small-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:small-rank">[trace:small-rank]</a>, <a href="#trace:large-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:large-rank">[trace:large-rank]</a>, <a href="#trace:separate-leg-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:separate-leg-comparison">[trace:separate-leg-comparison]</a>, and <a href="#trace:slow-requirement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:slow-requirement">[trace:slow-requirement]</a>: $$\begin{split}
 d_\lambda((u_{n,i}-\eta)_+)
 &\leq \frac{\gamma_j}{32K_{m(n)}}d_\tau(b_j)\\
 &\leq\frac12
      d_\lambda((z_nP_n-\gamma_j/32)_+).
 \end{split}$$ This includes $d_\tau(b_j)=0$. If $d_\tau(b_j)=\infty$, then <a href="#trace:large-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:large-rank">[trace:large-rank]</a> gives $d_\lambda(t_n)=\infty$, and <a href="#trace:separate-leg-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:separate-leg-comparison">[trace:separate-leg-comparison]</a>, with finite $K_{m(n)}$, forces the right side of <a href="#trace:uniform-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:uniform-gap">[trace:uniform-gap]</a> to be infinite. Thus <a href="#trace:uniform-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:uniform-gap">[trace:uniform-gap]</a> also holds in that case. Its coordinate set is independent of $\lambda$.

<a id="trace:coordinate-identifications"></a>
The element $P$ commutes with the entire first subsystem $B_1$. Also $q$ commutes with $\pi(A)$. Hence $\Psi$ is a homomorphism $\pi$ multiplied by commuting positive contractions, and is c.p.c. order zero. In $B$ we have $$\label{trace:coordinate-identifications}
 (u_{n,0})_\omega=e\pi(x_j),\qquad
 (u_{n,1})_\omega=e\theta(x_j),\qquad
 (z_nP_n)_\omega=\Psi(a_j).$$

Since $R$ is exact and $\mathcal Z$-stable, Lemma <a href="#prelim:half-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:half-comparison">9</a> applies to <a href="#trace:uniform-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:uniform-gap">[trace:uniform-gap]</a>. Thus, on an $\omega$-large set, $$(u_{n,i}-\eta)_+
   \precsim(z_nP_n-2c_j)_+.$$ We explain the passage to the ultrapower, because the norm bound on witnesses is essential. Fix $j$, a positive rational $\eta$, $i\in\{0,1\}$, and an accuracy $\epsilon>0$, and let $u_i=(u_{n,i})_\omega$. By Lemma <a href="#prelim:bounded-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:bounded-witness">12</a>, choose witnesses $r_n\in R$ on this large set such that $$\begin{split}
 \bigl\|r_n^*(z_nP_n-c_j)_+r_n-(u_{n,i}-\eta)_+\bigr\|
     &<\epsilon,\\
 \|r_n\|^2&\leq(1+\epsilon)/c_j.
 \end{split}$$ Set $r_n=0$ elsewhere. This is a bounded sequence, so it defines $r\in B$. By <a href="#trace:coordinate-identifications" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:coordinate-identifications">[trace:coordinate-identifications]</a>, $$\bigl\|r^*(\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+r
       -(u_i-\eta)_+\bigr\|\leq\epsilon.$$ As $\epsilon$ is arbitrary, each displayed source cut is Cuntz below $(\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+$. Letting positive rational $\eta$ decrease to zero proves <a href="#trace:small-part-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:small-part-comparison">[trace:small-part-comparison]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-trace-copies"></a>
<div id="trace:copies" class="lemma">

<a id="trace:ultrapower-copies"></a>
**Lemma 25**. *For every positive integer $L$ there are contractions $v_1,\ldots,v_L\in B$, represented entirely in the $\mathcal Z_2$ leg, such that $$\label{trace:ultrapower-copies}
 v_l^*v_{l'}=\delta_{ll'}P\qquad(1\leq l,l'\leq L).$$ In particular these elements commute with $B_1$, and hence with $\pi(A)$ and $q$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-trace-copies"><summary>Proof of stmt-trace-copies</summary>

*Proof.* At coordinates where $m(n)\geq L$, use the elements $v_{m(n),l}$ from Lemma <a href="#supplies:copies" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="supplies:copies">21</a>; at all other coordinates use zero. The representatives have norm at most one. Since $m(n)\to_\omega\infty$, their relations in <a href="#supplies:copy-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="supplies:copy-relations">[supplies:copy-relations]</a> give <a href="#trace:ultrapower-copies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:ultrapower-copies">[trace:ultrapower-copies]</a>. Their tensor-factor location gives the stated commutation. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:commutant"></a>
### The supporting map and its relative commutant

We retain the notation of the preceding section. In particular, $$R=A\otimes\mathcal Z_1\otimes\mathcal Z_2\otimes\mathcal Z_3,
 \qquad B=R_\omega,$$ where $A$ is nonzero, separable, unital and nuclear. The maps $\pi,\theta\colon A\to B$ have ranges in the first subsystem $(A\otimes\mathcal Z_1)_\omega$, with $\pi$ the constant first-factor homomorphism. The positive contractions $e,q=1-e$ satisfy <a href="#setup:common-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:common-equations">[setup:common-equations]</a>. The second-leg contraction $P$ defines $$\Psi(a)=P(q-1/2)_+\pi(a),\qquad a\in A.$$ We shall use both the cut comparisons <a href="#trace:small-part-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:small-part-comparison">[trace:small-part-comparison]</a> and the arbitrarily long finite families of second-leg elements in <a href="#trace:ultrapower-copies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:ultrapower-copies">[trace:ultrapower-copies]</a>. The constant third leg will remain available for the functional-calculus argument.

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### A supporting map preserving the relevant ideals

Put $h=\theta(1_A)$. An abstract supporting homomorphism for an order-zero map need not take values in its codomain. We therefore seek a c.p.c. order-zero map $\widehat\theta\colon A\to B$ with three roles. Its factorization $h\widehat\theta=\theta$ recovers the model and gives $h\widehat\theta(1_A)=h$, the support identity for $h$ in the second matrix corner below. The equality $q\widehat\theta=q\pi$ preserves the common supported part. Finally, pointwise ideal containment of $e\widehat\theta(a')$ and $e\pi(a')$ in $\operatorname{Ideal}_B(\Psi(a'))$ supplies the hypothesis for Gabe’s theorem, Theorem <a href="#prelim:domination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:domination">13</a>, to approximate the error maps by finite sums of compressions of $\Psi$.

<a id="stmt-support-map"></a>
<div id="support:map" class="proposition">

<a id="support:factorization"></a>
<a id="support:common"></a>
<a id="support:ideals"></a>
**Proposition 26** (A supporting map preserving ideals). *There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\widehat\theta\colon A\to B$ whose range commutes with the constant copy of $\mathcal Z_3$ and such that $$\begin{aligned}
 h\widehat\theta(a)=\widehat\theta(a)h&=\theta(a),
       \qquad a\in A,\label{support:factorization}\\
 [e,\widehat\theta(a)]&=0,
 \qquad q\widehat\theta(a)=q\pi(a),\qquad a\in A,
       \label{support:common}\\
 e\widehat\theta(a'),\ e\pi(a')&\in\operatorname{Ideal}_B(\Psi(a')),
       \qquad a'\in A_+.
       \label{support:ideals}
\end{aligned}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-support-map"><summary>Proof of stmt-support-map</summary>

<a id="support:root-comparison"></a>
*Proof.* Consider the order-zero root maps $\theta^{1/l}$, for $l\geq1$. They commute with $e$ and the constant third leg. Indeed, each value $\theta^{1/l}(a)$ is a norm limit of sandwiches of $\theta(a)$ by bounded continuous functions of $h$. One obtains these sandwiches by truncating $t^{(1/l-1)/2}$ from above; the resulting scalar weights converge uniformly to $t^{1/l}$ after multiplication by $t$. The same observation gives $$\label{support:root-comparison}
 e\theta^{1/l}(x_j)\precsim e\theta(x_j).$$ Here $e$ commutes with the values involved, so the sandwiches also approximate the positive element on the left.

Taking $a=1_A$ in <a href="#setup:common-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:common-equations">[setup:common-equations]</a> gives $qh=q$. Continuous functional calculus therefore gives $qh^{1/l}=q$, and the sandwich formula together with <a href="#setup:common-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="setup:common-equations">[setup:common-equations]</a> yields $$q\theta^{1/l}(a)=q\pi(a),\qquad a\in A.$$ Moreover, $$h\theta^{1/l}(a)=\theta^{1/l}(a)h\longrightarrow\theta(a)
 \quad(l\longrightarrow\infty),$$ by the uniform convergence of $t^{1+1/l}$ to $t$ on $[0,1]$.

For every fixed $j$, combining <a href="#support:root-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="support:root-comparison">[support:root-comparison]</a> with <a href="#trace:small-part-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trace:small-part-comparison">[trace:small-part-comparison]</a> gives $$e\theta^{1/l}(x_j)\precsim(\Psi(a_j)-c_j)_+,
 \qquad c_j=\gamma_j/64>0.$$ The bounded-witness estimate of Lemma <a href="#prelim:bounded-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:bounded-witness">12</a>, with the spare cut $c_j$, now shows that this positive element can be approximated arbitrarily closely by $r_j^*\Psi(a_j)r_j$, with <a id="support:witness-bound"></a>$$\label{support:witness-bound}
 \|r_j\|^2\leq 2/c_j.$$ The bound depends on $j$ but, crucially, not on $l$.

We apply the countable diagonalization principle of Lemma <a href="#prelim:epsilon-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prelim:epsilon-test">11</a>. To make the boundedness issue explicit, lift each root map to a sequence of c.p.c. maps into $R$ by the Choi–Effros lifting theorem. Keep fixed representatives of $e,q,h,\theta,\Psi$ and $\pi$. The tests impose the factorization and common-part identities on countable dense subsets of $A$, commutation with $e$ and a dense subset of the constant third leg, and order zero on a countable dense family of orthogonal pairs of positive contractions. For each $j$ include a witness variable with a fixed representative bound strictly larger than $\sqrt{2/c_j}$, and impose $$e\widehat\theta(x_j)=r_j^*\Psi(a_j)r_j.$$ Every finite list is approximately solvable: take $l$ sufficiently large for the factorization tests, and then use the witnesses above with sufficiently small errors. Their fixed bounds allow the ultrapower tests to be realized by bounded coordinate variables. The diagonalization produces a c.p.c. map satisfying all the tests. Continuity extends the identities and commutations to all the specified elements; the dense orthogonality tests make the map order zero. This proves <a href="#support:factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="support:factorization">[support:factorization]</a> and <a href="#support:common" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="support:common">[support:common]</a>, as well as the witness identities for all $j$.

It remains to extend ideal containment from the listed cuts in <a href="#model:tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="model:tests">[model:tests]</a> to an arbitrary positive contraction $a'\in A$. Such an $a'$ can be approximated arbitrarily closely by $x_j$ with $a_j\in\operatorname{Ideal}_A(a')$. To see this, choose $d$ in the fixed dense positive family close to $a'$, and a small positive rational $r>\|d-a'\|$. The image of $d$ in $A/\operatorname{Ideal}_A(a')$ has norm less than $r$, so $(d-r)_+\in\operatorname{Ideal}_A(a')$.
